Please to be quietly seated, and I will tell you all about it.” And he did tell them all about it; subsequently repeating his confession before quite a number of disgusted and cheaply sold spiritualists at the “New York Spiritual Lyceum.” The theory formed by one of the three investigators referred to, as to Paine’s method of moving the table, was singularly correct.
Whilst the family with whom Paine boarded was away, one day, in attendance at a funeral, he took up several of the floor boards of the back parlor, and on the under side of them affixed a lever, with a crosspiece at one end of it; and, in the ends of the crosspiece, bits of wire were inserted, the wire being just as far apart as the legs of the table to be moved. Small holes were made in the floorboards for the wire to come through to reach the table-legs. The other end of the lever came within an inch or two of the wall. When all the arrangements were completed, and the table being properly placed in order to move it, Mr. Paine had only to insert one end of a short iron rod in a hole in the heel of his boot, put the other end of the rod through a hole in the floor, just under the edge of the carpet near the wall, and then press the rod down upon the end of the lever.
The movements necessary in fixing the iron rod to its place were executed while he was picking up his handkerchief, that he had purposely dropped.
The middle of the lever was attached to the floor, and the end with the crosspiece, being the heavier, brought the other end close up against the floor, the wires in the crosspiece having their points just within the bottom of the holes in the floor. The room was carpeted, and there were little marks on the carpet, known only to Paine, that enabled him to know just where to place the table. Pressing down the end of the lever nearest the wall, an inch would bring the wires in the crosspiece on the other end of the lever against the legs of the table, and slightly raise the latter. One of the wires would strike the table-leg a very little before the other did, and that enabled the “medium” to very nicely rap time to the tunes that were sung or played. Of course, no holes that anyone could observe would be made in the carpet by the passage of the wires through it.
For appearance’ sake, Paine, before his detection, visited, by invitation, the houses of several different spiritualists, for the purpose of holding séances; but he never got a table to move “without contact” in any other than the place where he had properly prepared the conditions.

===Chinese===

当时在克林顿礼堂（Clinton Hall）举办的“周日招魂论大会”上，招魂论领袖们绘声绘色地讲述了佩恩先生所展示的“亡灵显灵”，并以信徒的身份为世间竟存在如此“铁证如山的事实”而彼此庆贺。那位在家中举办“无接触移桌”表演的招魂论者素以诚实严谨闻名；人们有理由推定，若非他本人知情，绝不可能有人在他家动用机械机关去搬动他的一件家具——毕竟那张桌子本就是他的私产——而要说他会纵容一场骗局，更是绝无可能。

纽约城里有三位先生，早先也曾以招魂论者自居；但在佩恩初登纽约灵媒舞台那阵子，他们对所谓“物质显灵”却已大起疑心。此前不久，他们刚刚当场揪出了达文波特兄弟及其他职业灵媒行骗的把柄；因此他们下定决心，在彻底查究佩恩的“显灵”、确认找不到任何物质成因之前，绝不轻信他的通灵说辞。连着出席了几场佩恩的降神会后，这三位先生断定佩恩是靠地板底下暗藏的机械机关挪动桌子的。三人中有一位本身就是机械师，他推想出了一种手法——在他看来也是当时表象之下唯一可能奏效的手法。佩恩同样是个机械师，他们三人对此心知肚明。于是他们约好佩恩单独举行一场私人降神会。到了约定当晚，三人来到他房间碰面。桌子照旧抬起，桌面上也照旧响起敲击声，与先前几场别无二致。三名查验者中的一人起身走到房门前，反手锁上门，把钥匙揣进衣兜，脱下外套，随后正色对佩恩先生说道：他决意对佩恩搜身，倘若没能从他身上搜出一根短小的铁棍——正是靠这根铁棍穿过地板小孔，去撬动底下的杠杆从而顶起桌子——那么说话人愿当场向佩恩先生赔礼道歉，并从此死心塌地信奉无形亡灵确能挪动有重之物的神力。这番掷地有声的简短宣言，对这位“灵媒”产生了立竿见影的奇效。“诸位先生，”佩恩开口道，“我还是坦白了吧。请诸位安坐，我把来龙去脉一五一十地全告诉你们。”他果然和盘托出；随后还在“纽约招魂学社”当着一大群既恶心又觉得自己被廉价戏弄了一把的招魂论者面前，重复了这番供词。那位查验者先前推测出的佩恩移桌机关，竟与事实惊人地吻合。

原来有一天，趁着佩恩寄宿的那户人家出门参加葬礼，他撬开了后客厅的几块地板，在地板背面装上一根杠杆，杠杆一端固定着一根横木；横木两端各插着一截铁丝，两根铁丝的间距恰好与待移动的桌子两条桌腿的间距相等。地板上钻了小孔，好让铁丝向上穿过地板顶住桌腿。杠杆的另一端则伸到离墙面仅一两英寸的地方。一切机关布置妥当、桌子也按恰当位置摆好后，佩恩先生只需把一根短铁棍的一头插进自己靴跟预先凿好的小孔里，另一头穿过墙角地毯边缘底下那处地板小孔，随后用脚往下一踩，压住杠杆末端即可。

把铁棍对准插入小孔的这番动作，全是他趁着俯身捡起自己故意掉在地上的手帕时顺手完成的。

杠杆中段固定在地板背面，带着横木的那一头较重，自然把另一头翘起来紧贴地板；横木上的铁丝尖端平时就缩在地板小孔底端。屋里铺着地毯，地毯上留有只有佩恩自己认得出的微小记号，好让他每次都能分毫不差地摆正桌子。只要把靠墙那一头的杠杆往下踩一英寸，另一头横木上的铁丝便会顶住桌腿，将桌子微微抬起。其中一根铁丝会比另一根早一丁点儿撞上桌腿，这恰好让“灵媒”得以随心所欲地跟着歌声或琴声漂亮地敲出节拍。至于细铁丝穿过地毯留下的针眼，自然谁也看不出来。

为了掩人耳目，佩恩在败露之前也曾应邀去过好几位不同招魂论者家中主持降神会；但除了他自己事先做足手脚的那间屋子，他在别处从未让任何一张桌子在“无接触”下挪动过半寸。

### XVI / 第十六章

===Original===

Spiritualist humbugs waking up﻿—​Foster heard from﻿—​S. B. Brittan heard from﻿—​The Boston artists and their spiritual portraits﻿—​The Washington medium and his spiritual hands﻿—​The Davenport brothers and the sea-captain’s wheat-flour﻿—​The Davenport brothers roughly shown up by John Bull﻿—​How a shingle “stumped” the spirits.
I hear from spiritualists sometimes. These gentry are much exercised in their minds by my letters about them, and some of them fly out at me very much as bumblebees do at one who stirs up their nest. For instance, I received, not long ago, from my good friends, Messrs. Cauldwell & Whitney, an anonymous letter to them, dated at Washington, and suggesting that if I would attend what the latter calls “a séance of that celebrated humbug, Foster,” I should see something that I could not explain. Now, this anonymous letter, as I know by a spiritual communication, (or otherwise,) is in a handwriting very wonderfully like that of Mr. Foster himself. And as for the substance of it, it is very likely that Foster has now gotten up some new tricks. He needs them. The exhibiting mediums must, of course, contrive new tricks as fast as Dr. Von Vleck and men like him show up their old ones. It is the universal method of all sorts of impostors to adopt new means of fooling people when their old ones are exposed. And Mr. Foster shall have all the attention he wants if I ever find the leisure to bestow on him, though my time is fully occupied with worthier objects.
I have also been complimented with a buzz and an attempt to sting from my old friend S. B. Brittan, the ex-Universalist minister﻿—the very surprisingly efficient “man Friday” of Andrew Jackson Davis, in the production of the “Revelations” of the said Davis, and also ghost-fancier in general; who has gently aired part of his vocabulary in a communication to the *Banner of Light*, with the heading “Exposed for Two Shillings.” I can afford very well to expose friend Brittan and his spiritualist humbugs for two shillings. The honester the cheaper. It evidently vexes the spiritualists to have their ghosts put with the monkeys in the Museum. They can’t help it, though; and it is my deliberate opinion that the monkeys are much the most respectable. I have no wish to displease any honest person; but the more the spiritualists squirm, and snarl, and scold, and call names, the more they show that I am hurting them. Or﻿—does my friend Brittan himself want an engagement at the Museum? Will he produce some “manifestations” there, and get that $500?﻿—the money is ready!
A valued friend of mine has furnished me a pleasant and true narrative of a fine “spiritual” humbug which took place in a respectable Massachusetts village not very long ago. I give the story in his own graphic words:
“Two artists of Boston, tired of the atmosphere of their studios, resolved themselves, in joint session, into spiritual mediums, as a means of raising the wind﻿—or the devil﻿—and of getting a little fresh air in the rural districts. One of them had learned Mansfield’s trick of answering communications and that of writing on the arms. They had large handbills printed, announcing that ‘Mr. W. Howard, the celebrated test-medium, would visit the town of —﻿—﻿—, and would remain at the —﻿—﻿— Hotel during three days.’ One of the artists preceded the other by a few hours, engaged rooms, and attended to sundry preliminaries. “Mr. Howard” donned a white choker, put his hair behind his ears, and mounted a pair of plain glass spectacles; and such was his profoundly spiritual appearance on entering his apartments at the hotel, that he had to lock the door and give his partner opportunity to explode, and absolutely roll about on the floor with laughter.

===Chinese===

招魂论骗子们坐不住了——福斯特来信——S. B. 布里顿发声——波士顿画家与他们的亡灵肖像——华盛顿灵媒与他的幽灵之手——达文波特兄弟与老船长的小麦面粉——达文波特兄弟遭约翰牛粗暴揭穿——一块木瓦片如何“难倒”亡灵。

我时不时会收到招魂论者的来信。这班老爷们对我写的那些揭批他们的文章心头着实火大，其中几个人朝我扑腾过来的架势，活像马蜂窝被人捅了一般。举个例子：前不久，我从好朋友考德威尔与惠特尼先生（Messrs. Cauldwell & Whitney）那儿转收到一封寄给他们的匿名信，落款地点是华盛顿，信中建议说：我若是肯去参加一趟信里所谓的“那位大名鼎鼎的欺罔大师福斯特（Foster）的降神会”，准能见识到一些我解释不了的奇事。巧的是，通过某种通灵感应（或者别的门道），我认出这封匿名信的笔迹竟与福斯特先生本人的字迹惊人地相似。至于信里的内容嘛，福斯特如今多半确实琢磨出了几手新花样。他也确实需要新花样。范弗莱克医生以及像他一样的人刚把旧把戏拆穿，登台献艺的灵媒们自然得赶紧编造新把戏。一旦旧法子败露，便立刻换上新手段继续愚弄世人，这是古往今来一切骗子的通用伎俩。日后我若得闲，定会如福斯特先生所愿好好关照他一番，尽管眼下我的时间全都忙着更有价值的正经事。

我还荣幸地领教了老朋友 S. B. 布里顿（S. B. Brittan）的一阵嗡嗡叫与蛰刺——这位前普救派牧师，正是安德鲁·杰克逊·戴维斯（Andrew Jackson Davis）炮制其《启示录》时那位效率惊人的得力干将“星期五”，兼业余赏鬼行家；他在《光明旗帜报》上发表了一篇题为《两先令揭穿骗局》的来信，彬彬有礼地亮出了他那套骂人词汇库里的部分存货。我花两先令揭穿老友布里顿和他的招魂论骗局，那是绰绰有余——越实诚越便宜嘛。把他们的鬼魂跟博物馆里的猴子摆在一块儿，显然叫招魂论者们气急败坏。可他们对此也无可奈何；依我审慎的看法，猴子可比他们体面得多。我无意冒犯任何诚实之人；不过招魂论者们越是扭来扭去、龇牙咧嘴、破口大骂、胡乱扣帽子，便越发证明我戳中了他们的痛处。还是说——我的朋友布里顿本人也想来博物馆签份演出合约？他可愿来这儿表演几手“显灵”，把那 500 美元奖金领走？——现钱早就备好了！

我的一位挚友向我讲述了一桩既生动又真实的好戏——不久前在马萨诸塞州一座体面村镇里上演的一场精彩“通灵”骗局。我就用他本人绘声绘色的原话复述如下：

“波士顿有两位画家，厌倦了画室里沉闷的空气，合议摇身一变成为通灵灵媒，既能借此弄点钱花——或者招点‘魔鬼’——又能去乡间呼吸些新鲜空气。其中一人学过曼斯菲尔德回信的把戏以及在手臂上显字的法子。他们印了大张招贴，宣布‘著名实证灵媒 W. 霍华德先生（Mr. W. Howard）即将造访——镇，并在——旅馆下榻三天’。其中一位画家提前几小时先行抵达，订好房间，打点好各项杂务。‘霍华德先生’系上白色硬高领结，把头发拢到耳后，架上一副平光眼镜；他走进旅馆房间时那副深沉超脱、仙风道骨的派头，逼得他赶紧反锁房门，好让搭档尽情爆发，简直在地板上笑得打滚。

===Original===

“Well, they rigged a clotheshorse for a screen; and to heighten the effect, the assistant, who was expert in portraiture, covered this screen, and, indeed, the walls of the room, with scraggy outlines of the human countenance upon large sheets of paper. These, they said, were executed by the draftsman, whose right hand, when under spiritual influence, uncontrollably jerked off these likenesses. They added, that the spirits had given information that, before the mediums left town, the people would recognize these pictures as likenesses of persons there deceased within twenty years or so. Price, two dollars each! They absolutely sold quite a large number of these portraits, as they were from time to time recognized by surviving friends! The operation of drawing portraits was also illustrated at certain hours, admission, fifty cents; if not satisfactory, the money returned.
“Other tricks of various kinds were performed with pleasure to all parties and profit to the performers. The artists stood it as long as they could, and then departed. But there was every indication that the townspeople would have stood it until this day.”
Thus far my friend’s curious and truthful account.
A little while ago, there was exhibiting, at Washington, a “test-medium” whose name I would print, were it not that I do not want to advertise him. One of his most impressive feats was, to cause spiritual hands and other parts of the human frame to appear in the air à la Davenport Brothers. A gentleman, whose name I also know very well indeed, but have particular reasons for not mentioning, went one day to see this “test-medium,” along with a friend, and asked to see a hand. “Certainly,” the medium said; and the room was darkened, and the “circle” made round the table in the usual manner. After about five minutes, my friend, who had contrived to place himself pretty near the medium, saw, sure enough, a dim glimmering blue light in the air, a foot or so before and above the head of the medium. In a minute, he could see, dimly outlined in this blue light, the form of a hand, back toward him, fingers together, and no thumb.
“Why is no thumb visible?” asked my friend of the medium in a solemn manner.
“The reason is,” said the medium, still more solemnly, “that the spirits have not power enough to produce a whole hand and so they exhibit as much as they can.”
“And do they always show hands without thumbs?”
“Yes.”

===Chinese===

“接着，他们拿一只晾衣架搭了架屏风；为了烘托气氛，擅长肖像画的助手用大张画纸画满了歪歪扭扭的人脸轮廓，贴满了整架屏风乃至房间的四壁。他们宣称，这些画作全是那位画师在受亡灵附体时，右手情不自禁、抽搐般疾挥而成的。他们还补充道，亡灵已经降下启示：在灵媒离开小镇之前，镇民们定会认出这些画像正是过去二十来年间在本地去世之人的肖像。每幅售价两美元！果不其然，由于在世亲友频频从画中认出了故人，他们居然实打实地卖出了好大一批这类肖像！他们还定时现场演示通灵作画的过程，门票五十美分；若不满意，原银奉还。

“此外他们还表演了各式各样的其他把戏，宾主尽欢，表演者也赚得盆满钵满。这两位画家实在憋笑憋到极限了，这才收拾行囊离去。可看当时的架势，镇上的居民怕是直到今天还愿意接着捧场呢。”

我朋友那段奇趣而又真实的故事到此为止。

前不久，华盛顿有一位“实证灵媒”登台献艺——若非不愿替他打免费广告，我倒真想把他的名字印出来。他最拿手的绝活之一，便是模仿达文波特兄弟的做派，让幽灵之手和人体其他部位凭空浮现在半空中。有一位绅士——他的姓名我也再清楚不过，只是因故不便提及——某天与一位朋友结伴去看这位“实证灵媒”，提出想看看幽灵之手。“当然可以，”灵媒答道；于是屋子熄灯变黑，众人照例围着桌子坐成“降神圈”。大约五分钟后，我那位设法挨着灵媒坐得很近的朋友，果真瞧见灵媒头顶上方约莫一英尺的前方半空中，泛起一团微弱闪烁的蓝光。过了一会儿，他在那团蓝光中隐约看出一只手的轮廓，手背朝向他，四指并拢，却没有大拇指。

“为什么看不见大拇指？”我朋友一本正经地问灵媒。

“原因是，”灵媒以更加肃穆的口吻答道，“亡灵的法力不足以变出一整只手，只能尽力显露这么多。”

“那他们每次现身的手都没有大拇指吗？”

“是的。”

===Original===

Here my friend, with a sudden jump, grabbed for the place where the wrist of the mysterious hand ought to be. Strange to relate, he caught it, and held it stoutly, to. A light was quickly had, when, still stranger, the spirit-hand was clearly seen to be the fleshy paw of the medium﻿—and a fat paw it was too. Mr. Medium took the matter with the coolness of a thorough rascal, and, lighting a cigar, merely observed:
“Well gentlemen, you needn’t trouble yourselves to come here any more!”
He also insisted on his usual fee of five dollars, until threatened with a prosecution for swindling.
The secret of this worthy gentleman is simple and soon told. Holding one hand up in the air, he held up with the other, between the thumb and finger, a little pinch of phosphorus and bi-sulphide of carbon, which gave the blue light. If inconvenient to hold up the other hand, he had a reserve pinch of blue-light under that invisible thumb. It is a curious instance of the thorough credulity of genuine spiritualists that a believer in this wretched rogue, on being circumstantially told this whole story, not only steadily and firmly refused to credit it, and continued his faith in the fellow, but absolutely would not go to see the application of any other test. That’s the sort of follower that is worth having!
Another case was witnessed as follows, by the very same person on whose authority I give the spirit-hand story. He was present﻿—also, this time in Washington, as it happened, at an exhibition by a certain pair of spiritual brothers, since well known as the “Davenport Brothers.”
These chaps, after the fashion of their kind, caused themselves to be tied up in a rope, an old sea-captain tying them. This done, their “shop” or cabinet, was shut upon them as usual, and the bangs, throwing of sticks, etc., through a window, and the like, took place. Well, this sly and inconvenient old sea-captain now slipped out of the hall a few minutes, and came back with some wheat flour. Having tied up the “brothers” again, he remarked:
“Now, gentlemen, please to take, each, your two hands full of wheat flour.”

===Chinese===

话音未落，我朋友猛地一跃而起，一把抓向那只神秘之手的手腕所在位置。说来也怪，他不仅抓了个正着，还死死攥住不放。屋里很快点起灯来，更奇的是，众人清清楚楚地瞧见那只“幽灵之手”原来正是灵媒本人肉呼呼的大胖手——而且还真够肥的。这位灵媒先生却拿出了十足老千的镇定劲儿，点上一支雪茄，只是淡淡说道：

“得，诸位先生，往后就不必劳驾再上这儿来了！”

他甚至还死缠着要收惯常的五美元出场费，直到众人威胁要以诈骗罪起诉他，这才作罢。

这位可敬先生的秘密简单得很，三言两语就能道破。他把一只手举在半空，另一只手用拇指和食指捏着一小撮磷与二硫化碳的混合物举到近旁，便发出了那团蓝光。要是不方便同时举起另一只手，他就在那根“看不见的大拇指”底下藏了一小撮备用的蓝光药粉。真正招魂论者的彻底盲信，从下面这件怪事可见一斑：有一位信徒，别人把这整桩经过详详细细讲给他听，他非但咬紧牙关坚决不信、继续死忠追随这个卑劣无赖，甚至根本不肯去看任何其他实证测验。这号信徒可真是值得拥有啊！

另一桩事也是由讲出“幽灵之手”故事的同一位可靠证人亲眼目睹的。巧的是，这次同样是在华盛顿，他出席了一场由某对通灵兄弟举办的表演——这对兄弟后来便以“达文波特兄弟”的名号闻名于世。

这两个小子照他们那一行的老套路，让人拿绳子把自己捆起来，动手捆绑的是一位老船长。捆好之后，照例关上他们的“作坊”或称降神柜，里头随即响起砰砰巨响，小窗口里扔出木棍等等，与往常如出一辙。然而，这位狡黠又难缠的老船长悄悄溜出大厅几分钟，回来时手里多了些小麦面粉。他再次把“兄弟俩”捆结实后，开口说道：

“好了，两位先生，请劳驾各自两只手都满满攥上一把小麦面粉吧。”

===Original===

The “brothers” got mad and flatly refused. Then they cooled down and argued, saying it wouldn’t make any difference, and was of no use.
“Well,” said the ancient mariner, “if it won’t make any difference you can just as well do it, can’t you?”
The audience, seeing the point, were so evidently pleased with the old sailor, that the grumbling “brothers” though with a very bad grace, took their fists full of flour, and were shut up.
There was not the least sign of a “manifestation”﻿—no more than if the wheat-flour had shot the “brothers” dead in their tracks. The audience were immensely delighted. The “brothers,” since that time, have learned to perform some tricks with flour in their fists, but only when tied by their own friends.
Since these facts came to my knowledge, the Davenport Brothers have suffered an unpleasant exposure in Liverpool, in England, the details of which have been kindly forwarded to me by attentive friends there. The circumstances in question occurred on the evenings of Tuesday and Wednesday, February 14 and 15, 1865. On the first of these evenings, a gentleman named Cummins, selected by the audience as one of the Tying Committee, tied one of the Brothers, and a Mr. Hulley, the other committeeman, the other. But the Brothers saw instantly that they could not wriggle out of these knots. They, therefore, refused to let the tying be finished, saying that it was “brutal” although a surgeon present said it was not; one tied brother was untied by Ferguson, the agent; and then the Brothers went to work and performed their various tricks without the supervision of any committee, but amid a constant fire of derision, laughter, groans, shouts, and epithets from the audience. On the next evening, the audience insisted on having the same committee; the Brothers were very reluctant to allow it, but had to do so after a long time. Ira Davenport refused again, however, instantly to be tied, as soon as he saw what knot Mr. Cummins was going to use. Cummins, however, though Ira squirmed most industriously, got him tied fast, and then Ira called to Ferguson to cut the knot! Ferguson did so, and cut Ira’s hand. Ira now showed the blood to the audience, and the Brothers, with an immense pretense of indignation, went off the stage. Cummins at once explained; the audience became disgusted, and, enraged at the impudence of the imposture, broke over the footlights, knocked Ferguson backward into the “cabinet;” and when the discomfited agent had scrambled out and run away, smashed the thing fairly into kindling-wood, and carried it off, all distributed into splinters and chips. Early next morning, the terrified Davenports ran away out of Liverpool; and a number of the audience were, at last accounts, intending to go to law to get back the money paid for an exhibition which they did not see.
The very thorough exposure of the Davenports thus made is an additional proof﻿—if such were needed﻿—of the truth of what I have alleged about the impostures perpetrated by them and their “mysterious” brethren of the exhibiting sort.
Once the “spirits” were “stumped” with a shingle﻿—a very proper yankee jawbone of an ass to route such disembodied Philistines. One day a certain person was present where some tables were rambling about, and other revolutions taking place in the furniture-business, when he stepped boldly forth like a herald bearing defiance, and cast down a common white pine shingle upon the floor. “There,” said he, coolly, “if you can trot those tables about in that style, do it with that shingle. Make it go about the room. Make it move an inch!” And lo, and behold! the shingle lay perfectly still.

===Chinese===

“兄弟俩”顿时火冒三丈，一口回绝。随后他们冷静下来，又百般狡辩，说攥不攥面粉根本毫无区别，纯属多此一举。

“那好啊，”老水手说道，“既然毫无区别，那你们照办也无妨，对吧？”

观众们立刻听出了弦外之音，对这位老水手大加赞赏；“兄弟俩”满腹牢骚、万般不情愿，也只得各自攥了满满两拳头面粉，被关进了柜子。

结果连半点儿“显灵”的影子也没有——那效果简直跟小麦面粉当场把“兄弟俩”击毙在原地一样。观众们乐坏了。从那以后，“兄弟俩”倒是学会了手里攥着面粉还能耍几手把戏，但前提是必须由他们自己的熟人来捆绳。

自打我得知上述事实以来，达文波特兄弟又在英格兰利物浦遭遇了一场颇为难堪的当众揭穿，当地热心的朋友已将详情惠寄于我。事情发生在 1865 年 2 月 14 日（星期二）和 15 日（星期三）晚间。头一天晚上，由观众推选出的捆绳委员会成员之一、名叫卡明斯（Cummins）的绅士负责捆绑其中一位兄弟，另一位委员赫利先生（Mr. Hulley）负责捆绑另一位。可兄弟俩一眼就看出自己绝无可能从这些绳结里扭脱出来。于是他们拒绝让捆绑继续下去，声称这太“野蛮”，尽管在场的一位外科医生表示根本不野蛮；已被捆住的那位兄弟由经纪人弗格森（Ferguson）解开；随后兄弟俩在没有任何委员会监督的情况下自顾自地表演了各种把戏，但全场自始至终充斥着观众的嘲弄、哄笑、嘘声、叫喊与辱骂。第二天晚上，观众坚持要求仍由同一批委员上台捆绑；兄弟俩百般推诿，僵持良久后才不得不勉强同意。然而，艾拉·达文波特（Ira Davenport）刚一瞧见卡明斯先生准备打哪种绳结，立刻再次拒绝被捆。尽管艾拉拼命扭动挣扎，卡明斯还是手脚利落地把他捆了个结结实实；紧接着，艾拉高喊弗格森上来割断绳结！弗格森照办时，顺带割伤了艾拉的手。艾拉随即向观众展示流血的手掌，兄弟俩摆出一副受了奇耻大辱的愤慨姿态，拂袖退场。卡明斯立刻向全场说明了原委；观众们厌恶至极，对这伙骗子厚颜无耻的行径怒不可遏，纷纷翻越脚灯冲上舞台，把弗格森仰面朝天掀翻进“降神柜”里；待狼狈不堪的经纪人爬出来逃之夭夭后，众人把那座柜子砸了个稀巴烂，劈成一地碎木柴，各自捡了一把木屑碎块作纪念带走了。次日清晨，吓破了胆的达文波特兄弟便仓皇逃离了利物浦；据最新消息，不少观众正打算诉诸法律，讨回那场压根儿没看成的演出门票钱。

达文波特兄弟此番遭到如此彻底的揭穿，无疑为我此前所指证的他们及其“神秘”同行在舞台上施展的种种骗局，又添了一记铁证——假使这还用得着添证的话。

还有一回，“亡灵”竟被一块木瓦片给“难倒”了——这可真是一块专治此类无形非利士人的地道扬基“驴腮骨”。某天，有个人在场观看几张桌子四处乱窜、家什家具闹翻了天的场面，他像一位下战书的传令官般大步上前，把一块寻常的白松木瓦片往地板上一扔。“瞧着，”他冷冷地说道，“你们要真有本事让那些桌子满屋子溜达，就拿这块木瓦片试试。让它在屋里转一圈！让它挪动哪怕一英寸！”结果你瞧怎么着——那块木瓦片纹丝不动地躺在那儿。

### XVII / 第十七章

===Original===

The Davenport brothers shown up once more﻿—​Dr. Newton at Chicago﻿—​The spiritualist bogus baby﻿—​A lady brings forth a motive force﻿—​“Gum” arabic﻿—Spiritualist hebrew﻿—​The Allen boy﻿—​Dr. Randall﻿—​Portland Evening Courier﻿—​The fools not all dead yet.
Other “spiritual” facts have come to my hand, some of them furnishing additional details about persons to whom I have already alluded, and others being important to illustrate some general tendencies of spiritualism.
And first, about the Davenport Brothers; they have met with another “awful exposure,” at the hands of a merciless Mr. Addison. This gentleman is a London stockbroker, and his cool, sharp business habits seem to have stood him in good stead in taking some fun out of the fools who follow the Davenports. Mr. Addison, it seems, went to work, and, just to amuse his friends, executed all the Davenport tricks. Upon this the spiritualist newspapers in England, which, like the *Boston Herald of Progress*, claim to believe in the “Brothers,” came out and said that Addison was a very wonderful medium indeed. On this the cold-blooded Addison at once printed a letter, in which he not only said he had done all their tricks without spiritual aid, but he moreover explained exactly how he caught the Davenports in their impositions. He and a long-legged friend went to one of the “dark séances” of the Davenports, during which musical instruments were to fly about over the heads of the audience, bang their pates, thrum, twang, etc. Addison and his friend took a front seat; as soon as the lights were put out they put out their legs too; stretching as far as possible; and, to use the unfeeling language of Mr. Addison, they “soon had the satisfaction of feeling someone falling over them.” They then caught hold of an arm, from which a guitar was forthwith let drop on the floor. In order to be certain who the guitar-carrier was, they waited until the next time the lights were put out, took each a mouthful of dry flour, and blew it out right among the “manifestations.” When the lamps were lighted, lo and behold! there was Fay, the agent and manager of the Davenports, with his back all powdered with flour. Addison showed this to an acquaintance, who said, “Yes, he saw the flour; but he could not understand what made Addison and his friend laugh so excessively at it.”
The spiritualist newspapers don’t think Addison is so great a medium as they did!
Great accounts have recently come eastward from Chicago, of a certain Doctor Newton, who is said to be working miracles by the hundred in the way of healing diseases. This man operates with exactly the weapons all the miracle-workers, quacks, and impostors, ancient and modern use. All of them have appealed to the imaginations of their patients, and no person acquainted with mental philosophy is ignorant that many a sickman has been cured either by medicine and imagination together, or by imagination alone. Therefore, even if this Newton should really be the cause of the recovery of some persons from their ailments, it would be no more a miracle than if Dr. Mott should do it; nor would Newton be any the less a quack and a humbug.
Newton has operated at the East already. He had a career at New Haven and Hartford, and in other places, before he steered westward in the wake of the “Star of Empire.” What he does is simply to ask what is the matter, and where it hurts. Then he sticks his thumb into the seat of the difficulty, or he pokes or strokes or pats it, as the case may be. Then he says, “There﻿—you’re cured! God bless you!﻿—Take yourself off!”

===Chinese===

达文波特兄弟再度穿帮——芝加哥的牛顿医生——招魂论者的假婴儿——一位女士生出一股“原动力”——“阿拉伯”树胶——招魂论者的希伯来语——艾伦男孩——兰德尔医生——《波特兰晚邮报》——世上的傻瓜尚未死绝。

我手头又搜集到一批“通灵”实事，其中有些补充了我先前已提过的人物细节，另一些则足以生动说明招魂论的若干普遍流弊。

先说说达文波特兄弟；他们近来在一位毫不留情的艾迪生先生（Mr. Addison）手里又栽了个“大跟头”。这位先生是伦敦的一名证券经纪人，他那冷静敏锐的商场作风，在戏耍追捧达文波特兄弟的傻瓜时似乎派上了大用场。据说艾迪生先生为了逗朋友们一乐，亲自动手把达文波特兄弟的全套把戏演了一遍。英格兰那些招魂论报纸——就像《波士顿进步先驱报》（*Boston Herald of Progress*）一样自称笃信“兄弟俩”——见状立刻跳出来吹捧，说艾迪生着实是一位了不起的灵媒。冷酷无情的艾迪生闻讯当即刊登了一封公开信，不仅声明自己全套把戏未借助半点亡灵之力，更一五一十地讲清了自己是如何当场揪住达文波特兄弟行骗把柄的。他和一位大长腿朋友去参加达文波特兄弟的一场“暗室降神会”，照例要在会上让乐器在观众头顶乱飞、敲打脑壳、叮咚弹拨。艾迪生和朋友挑了前排座位；灯光一灭，他俩也把腿伸了出去，能伸多长伸多长；借用艾迪生先生那句毫无温情的原话，他们“很快便心满意足地感觉到有人被绊倒在他们腿上”。紧接着他们一把抓住了一条胳膊，那只胳膊手里拎着的一把吉他当场掉在地板上。为了确证拎吉他的究竟是谁，他俩等到下一次熄灯时，各自含了一大口干面粉，对着“显灵”闹腾的正中央猛地喷了出去。灯光重新亮起时，你瞧怎么着！达文波特兄弟的经纪人兼经理费伊（Fay）赫然站在那儿，后背沾满了白面粉。艾迪生把这一幕指给一位熟人看，那人却说：“是啊，我看见面粉了；可我不明白这有什么好让艾迪生和他的朋友笑得那么狂的。”

这下那些招魂论报纸可不觉得艾迪生是什么了不起的大灵媒了！

近来芝加哥向东传来不少轰动消息，说的是一位名叫牛顿医生（Doctor Newton）的人物，据说他在治病方面行过成百上千次神迹。这人使的手段，与古往今来所有行神迹者、江湖郎中与骗子如出一辙。他们无一不是诉诸病人的想象力，而稍通心智哲学的人都知道，世上不知有多少病人是靠药物与想象力共同作用、甚至单靠想象力便痊愈的。因此，即便这位牛顿真能让几个人摆脱病痛，那也绝不比莫特医生（Dr. Mott）治好病人更像神迹；牛顿也丝毫不因此减少半点江湖郎中与骗子（humbug）的成色。

牛顿早先已在东部施展过拳脚。他在追随“帝国之星”西进之前，就曾在纽黑文、哈特福德等地混过一番名堂。他的做法简单得很，无非是问一声哪里不舒服、哪儿疼。接着便把大拇指往病灶处一戳，或者视情况捅一捅、抚摩几下、拍打几下。随后说道：“好了——你痊愈了！愿上帝保佑你！——走吧！”

===Original===

Chicago must be a credulous place, for we are informed of immense crowds besieging this man, and undergoing his manipulations. One of the Chicago papers, having little faith and a good deal of fun﻿—which in such cases is much better﻿—published some burlesque stories and certificates about “Doctor” Newton, some of them humorous enough. There is a certificate from a woman with fourteen children, all having the measles at once. She says that no sooner had Doctor Newton received one lock of hair of one of them, than the measles left them all, and she now has said measles corked up in a bottle! Another case was that of a merchant who had lost his strength, but went and was stroked by Newton, and the very next day was able to lift a note in bank, which had before been altogether too heavy for him. There was also an old lady, whose story I fear was imitated from Hood’s funny conceit of the deaf woman who bought an ear-trumpet, which was so effective that

> —﻿—“The very next day
> 
> She heard from her husband in Botany Bay!”

The Chicago old lady in like manner, after having had Doctor Newton’s thumbs “jobbed” into her ears, certifies that she heard next morning from her son in California.
One would think that this ridicule would put the learned Dr. Newton to flight; but it will not until he is through with the fools.
I have already given an account of some of the messages from the other world in the “Banner of Light,” in which some of the spirits explain that they have turned into women since they died. This is by no means the first remarkable trick that the spirits have performed upon the human organization. Here is what they did at High Rock, in Massachusetts, a number of years ago. It beats Joanna Southcott in funny absurdity, if not in blasphemy.

===Chinese===

芝加哥必定是个极易轻信的地方，因为据报导，大批民众把这人围得水泄不通，争相接受他的推拿捏按。芝加哥当地有一家报纸对此毫无信仰却颇具幽默感——遇到这种事，幽默感可比信仰管用多了——刊登了一批拿“牛顿医生”开涮的讽刺故事与假证明书，其中有几则着实风趣。譬如有一份证明书来自一位有十四个孩子的母亲，十四个孩子同时得了麻疹。她说牛顿医生刚收下其中一孩的一绺头发，麻疹便瞬间从所有孩子身上消退了，她如今正把那些麻疹用软木塞封在一只瓶子里呢！另一桩病例是一位元气大伤的商人，找牛顿抚摩之后，第二天便能亲手“扛起”银行里一张此前他根本搬不动的期票（译注：英文 lift a note 兼有“举起”与“兑付/赎回票据”双关义）。还有一位老太太，她的故事我怀疑是仿照胡德（Hood）那段著名笑谈编出来的——那位耳背的妇人买了只助听喇叭，效果好到：

> ——“就在第二天，
> 
> 她便收到了远在植物湾（Botany Bay）的丈夫的音讯！”

芝加哥那位老太太也如出一辙，被牛顿医生把大拇指“戳”进耳朵眼儿之后，作证说第二天早晨就收到了加利福尼亚儿子的来信。

人们或许以为这番冷嘲热讽早该把博学的牛顿医生吓跑了；可只要他还没把那帮傻瓜榨干，他是绝不会收手的。

前文我已介绍过《光明旗帜报》上几则来自阴间的灵讯，其中有些亡灵解释说自己死后变成了女人。这绝非亡灵头一回在人体构造上大动干戈。下面要讲的，便是好几年前他们在马萨诸塞州高岩（High Rock）干下的奇事。论荒唐可笑，这桩事比乔安娜·索斯科特（Joanna Southcott）有过之而无不及，虽说亵渎神明的程度兴许略逊一筹。

===Original===

At High Rock, in the year 1854 or thereabouts, certain spiritualist people were building some mysterious machinery. While this was in process of erection, a female medium, of considerable eminence in those parts, was informed by certain spirits, with great solemnity and pomp, that “she would become the Mary of a new dispensation;” that is, she was going to be a mother. Well, this was all proper, no doubt, and the lady herself﻿—so say the spiritualist accounts﻿—had for some time experienced indications that she was pregnant. These indications continued, and became increasingly obvious, and also, it was observed, a little queer in some particulars.
After a while, one Spear﻿—a “Reverend Mr. Spear”﻿—who was mixed up, it appears, with the machinery-part of the business, and who was a medium himself, transmitted to the lady a request from the spirits that she would visit said Spear at High Rock on a certain day. She did so, of course; and while there was unexpectedly taken with the pains of childbirth, which the spiritualist authorities say, were “internal”﻿—where should they be, pray?﻿—and “of the spirit rather than of the physical nature; but were, nevertheless, quite as uncontrollable as those of the latter, and not less severe.” The labor proceeded. It lasted two hours. As it went on, lo and behold! one part and another part of the machinery began to move! And when, at the end of the two hours, the parturition was safely over, all the machinery was going!
The lady had given birth to a Motive Force. Does anybody suppose I am manufacturing this story? Not a bit of it. It is all told at length in a book published by a spiritualist; and probably a good many of my readers will remember about it.
Well, the baby had to be nursed﻿—fact! This superhumanly silly female actually went through the motions of nursing the motive force for some weeks. Though how the thing sucked﻿—Excuse me, ladies; I would not discuss such delicate subjects did not the interests of truth require it.
If I had been the physician, at any rate, I think I should have recommended to hire a healthy female steam-engine for a wet nurse to this young motive force; say a locomotive, for instance. I feel sure the thing would have lived if it could have had a gauge-faucet or something of that sort to draw on. But the medical folks in charge chose to permit the mother to nurse the child, and she not being able to supply proper nutriment, the poor little innocent faded﻿—if that word be appropriate for what couldn’t be seen﻿—and finally “gin eout;” and the machinery, after some abortive joggles and turns, stood hopelessly still.

===Chinese===

大约在 1854 年前后，马萨诸塞州高岩有一群招魂论者正在搭建一套神秘机器。机器架设期间，当地一位颇负盛名的女灵媒接到某些亡灵极其庄严隆重的启示，称“她将成为新世代的圣母玛利亚”——也就是说，她即将身怀六甲。好吧，这倒也顺理成章；而那位女士本人——据招魂论者的记载说——也确实有一阵子感觉到了怀孕的征兆。这些征兆持续不断，日渐明显，而且旁人也察觉到在某些细节上显得有点儿古怪。

过了一段时间，一位名叫斯皮尔（Spear）的人——人称“斯皮尔牧师”——此人似乎正深度参与那套机器的筹建，且他本人也是个灵媒，向那位女士转达了亡灵的指令，要她于某日前往高岩拜访这位斯皮尔。她自然遵命前往；就在逗留期间，她出乎意料地发作了阵痛，据招魂论权威人士称，这种阵痛是“内在的”——试问阵痛不在肚子里还能在哪儿呢？——而且“属于灵性而非肉体性质；尽管如此，却与肉体分娩一般难以自控，剧烈程度也丝毫不减”。产程开始了，足足持续了两个小时。随着产程推进，你瞧怎么着！那台机器的这个部件、那个部件竟接二连三地转动起来！等到两个小时后分娩平安结束，整台机器已然轰隆运转起来！

这位女士居然生出了一股“原动力”（Motive Force）。难道有人以为这故事是我胡诌的吗？绝无此事。这整段奇闻详详细细地印在一位招魂论者出版的书里；想必我的不少读者至今还记得这档子事。

既然生下来了，这宝宝总得喂奶吧——千真万确！这个愚蠢得超凡脱俗的女人，居然真的连续几周比划着哺乳这股“原动力”的动作。至于那玩意儿究竟是怎么吮吸的——诸位女士恕罪，若非为了捍卫真理，我绝不愿谈论如此细腻私密的话题。

总之，若当时由我来当主治医生，我想我定会建议雇一台健壮的母蒸汽机来给这股年幼的原动力当奶妈；譬如一辆火车头就很不错。我敢断定，要是它能有个水位计旋塞之类的东西吸上两口，准能活下来。可惜当时管事的医护人员偏偏允许生母亲自哺乳，而她又实在分泌不出恰当的乳汁，于是那可怜的小无辜便日渐消瘦凋零——如果“凋零”这词能用来形容一个压根儿看不见的东西——最终“油尽灯枯”；而那台机器在徒劳地抖动、空转了几下之后，也彻底绝望地停摆了。

===Original===

This story is true﻿—that is, it is true that the story was told, the pretences were gone through, and the birth was actually believed by a good many people. Some of them were prodigiously enthusiastic about it, and called the invisible brat the New Motive Power, the Physical Savior, Heaven’s Last Best Gift to Man, the New Creation, the Great Spiritual Revelation of the Age, the Philosopher’s Stone, the Act of all Acts, and so on, and so forth.
The great question of all was, Who was the daddy? I don’t know of anybody’s asking this question, but its importance is extreme and obvious. For if things like this are going to happen, the ladies will be afraid to sleep alone in the house if so much as a sewing-machine or apple-corer be about, and will not dare take solitary walks along any stream where there is a water power.
A couple of miscellaneous anecdotes may not inappropriately be appended to this story of monstrous delusion.
Once a “writing medium” was producing sentences in various foreign languages. One of these was Arabic. An enthusiastic youth, a half-believer, after inspecting the wondrous scroll, handed it to his seat-mate, a professor (as it happened) in one of our oldest colleges, and a man of real learning. The professor scrutinized the document. What was the youth’s delight to hear him at last observe gravely, “It is a kind of Arabic, sure enough!”
“What kind?” asked the young man with intense interest.
“Gum-arabic,” said the professor.

===Chinese===

这个故事千真万确——也就是说，确确实实有人讲述过这个故事，那套装神弄鬼的把戏确实演了一遍，而且还真有好些人对这次“分娩”深信不疑。其中有些人对此狂热至极，给那个看不见的“小屁孩”起了一大堆尊号，诸如“新原动力”、“物质救世主”、“上苍赐予人类的最后至宝”、“新创世”、“当代最伟大的灵界启示”、“贤者之石”、“万行之行”，林林总总，不一而足。

眼下最核心的问题是：孩子的爹是谁？我不知道可曾有人问过这个问题，但它的重要性再显赫不过。因为照这样发展下去，女士们哪怕屋里只放着一台缝纫机或一只苹果去核器，也会吓得不敢独自在家睡觉，更不敢沿着任何装有水轮动力的小溪独自散步了。

在这桩骇人听闻的妄想奇案末尾，附上一两则零星轶事倒也颇为相宜。

有一回，某位“书写灵媒”正奋笔疾书各种外语语句，其中一种据称是阿拉伯语（Arabic）。一位半信半疑的热血青年端详过那幅神奇的卷轴后，将其递给邻座——恰巧那是我国历史最悠久的高等学府之一的一位真才实学的教授。教授仔细审视了那份手稿。当他终于神色肃穆地开口道：“这确实是一种阿拉伯语（Arabic）！”时，那位青年别提多兴奋了。

“哪一种？”年轻人兴致勃勃地追问道。

“阿拉伯树胶（Gum-arabic），”教授答道。

===Original===

The spirit of the prophet Daniel came one night into the apartment of a medium named Fowler, and right before his eyes, he said, wrote down some marks on a piece of paper. These were shown to the Reverend George Bush, Professor of Hebrew in the New-York University, who said that they were “a few verses from the last chapter of Daniel” and were learnedly written. Bush was a spiritualist as well as a professor of Hebrew, and he ought to have known better than to endorse spirit-Hebrew; for shortly there came others, who, to use a rustic phrase, “took the rag off the Bush.” These inconvenient personages were three or four persons of learning: one a Jew, who proved that the document was an attempt to copy the verses in question, by someone so ignorant of Hebrew as not to know that it is written backward, that is, from right to left.
During the last few months, a “boy medium,” by the name of Henry B. Allen, thirteen years of age, has been astonishing people in various parts of the country by “Physical Manifestations in the Light.” The exhibitions of this precocious youngster have been “managed” by a Dr. Randall, who also lectures upon Spiritualism, expounding its “beautiful philosophy.” For a number of weeks this couple held forth in Boston, sometimes giving several séances during the day, not more than thirty being allowed to attend at one time, each of whom were required to pay an admission fee of one dollar.
*The Banner of Light* fully endorsed this Allen boy, and gave lengthy accounts of his manifestations. The arrangements for his exhibition were very simple. A dulcimer, guitar, bell, and small drum being placed on a sofa or several chairs set against the wall, a clotheshorse was set in front of them and covered with a blanket, which came to the floor. To obtain “manifestations,” a person was required to take off his coat and sit with his back to the clotheshorse. The medium then took a seat close to, and facing the investigator’s left side, and grasped the left arm of the latter on the under side, above the elbow, with his (the medium’s) right hand and near the wrist with the other hand. The “manager” then covered with a coat, the arms and left shoulder of the medium including the left arm of the investigator. The medium soon commenced to wriggle and twist﻿—the “manager” said he was always nervous under “influence”﻿—and worked the coat away from the position in which it had been placed. Taking his right hand from the investigator’s arm, he readjusted the coat, and availed himself of that opportunity to get the investigator’s wrist between his (the medium’s) left arm and knee. That brought his left hand in such a position that with it he could grasp the investigator’s arm where he had previously grasped it with his right hand. With the latter he could then reach around the edge of the clotheshorse and make a noise on the instruments. With the drumsticks he thumped on the dulcimer. Taking the guitar by the neck, he could vibrate the strings and show the body of the instrument above the clotheshorse, without anyone seeing his hand! All persons present were so seated that they could not see behind the clotheshorse, or have a view of the medium’s right shoulder. When asked why people were not allowed to occupy such a position, that they could have a fair view of the instruments when sounded, the “manager” replied that he did not exactly know, but presumed it was because the magnetic emanations from the eyes of the beholders would prevent the spirits being able to move the instruments at all! What was claimed to be a spirit-hand was often shown above the clotheshorse, where it flickered for an instant and was withdrawn; but it was invariably a right hand with the wrist toward the medium. When the person sitting with the medium was asked if the hands of the latter had constantly hold of his arm, he replied in the affirmative. Of course, he felt what he supposed to be both the medium’s hands; but as I before explained, the pressure on his wrist was from the medium’s left arm﻿—the left hand of whom, by means of a very accommodating crook in the elbow, was grasping the investigator’s arm where the medium’s right hand was supposed to be.

===Chinese===

有一晚，先知但以理的亡灵降临到一位名叫福勒（Fowler）的灵媒寓所，据福勒说，亡灵就在他眼皮底下在一张纸上写下了几个符号。这些符号被拿去给纽约大学希伯来语教授乔治·布什牧师（Reverend George Bush）过目，牧师鉴定称那是“《但以理书》最后一章的几节经文”，且写得极有学问。布什既是希伯来语教授，又是招魂论者，按理说他本不该贸然替这种“亡灵希伯来文”背书；因为没过多久，便来了几位较真的人，用句乡下俗话说，彻底“撕下了布什的遮羞布”。这几个大煞风景的人物是三四位真正的学者，其中一位还是犹太人；他们当场证明，那份文稿不过是某个人照猫画虎抄写那几节经文的结果——此人对希伯来语一窍不通，甚至不知道希伯来文是从右往左倒着写的。

近几个月来，一位年仅十三岁、名叫亨利·B. 艾伦（Henry B. Allen）的“少年灵媒”，凭藉“光天化日下的物质显灵”在全国各地频频令世人惊叹。这位早熟少年的演出由一位兰德尔医生（Dr. Randall）“经纪打理”，此人还顺带宣讲招魂论，阐发其“美妙哲学”。这老少二人曾在波士顿连续开坛好几周，有时一天连办好几场降神会，每次限额不超过三十人，每人须缴一美元门票。

《光明旗帜报》对这个艾伦男孩全力背书，连篇累牍地报道他的显灵神迹。他演出的场地布置极其简单：靠墙摆一张长沙发或几把椅子，上面放一架洋琴、一把吉他、一只铃铛和一面小鼓；前面支一架晾衣架，披上一条垂到地面的毯子。要召唤“显灵”，观众须脱下外套，背对着晾衣架坐下。随后灵媒紧挨着查验者左侧面对面坐下，用自己的右手从下方握住查验者手肘上方的左臂，用左手握住其手腕附近。“经理”再用一件外套盖住灵媒的双臂与左肩，连同查验者的左臂一并遮住。很快，灵媒便开始扭来扭去——“经理”解释说他一受“灵力感应”总是浑身紧张——趁机把盖在身上的外套蹭偏原位。于是他从查验者胳膊上抽回右手去整理外套，并借此机会把查验者的手腕夹进自己（灵媒）的左臂与膝盖之间。这样一来，他的左手便顺势挪到了先前右手所握的位置，得以牢牢抓住查验者的胳膊。腾出来的右手便可绕过晾衣架边缘，去敲打那几件乐器。他用鼓槌敲响洋琴，抓住吉他琴颈拨动琴弦，甚至把吉他琴身举到晾衣架上方露个脸，而旁人根本看不见他的手！在场所有人的座位都经过精心安排，既看不见晾衣架背后，也瞧不见灵媒的右肩。每当有人问起为什么不许观众坐到能清楚看见乐器发声的位置，“经理”便答称自己也不太清楚，但推测是因为观众眼中散发出的磁力射线会彻底妨碍亡灵挪动乐器！晾衣架上方还常常伸出一只号称是“幽灵之手”的东西，晃悠一瞬便缩回去；可那永远是一只右手，且手腕朝向灵媒本人。每当有人询问与灵媒同坐的查验者，灵媒的双手是否自始至终抓着他的胳膊，他总答说是的。他自然觉得自己一直感觉到灵媒的两只手；但正如我前文所解释的，压在他手腕上的其实是灵媒的左臂——而灵媒的左手借着胳膊肘一个极其顺手的弯曲，正抓在原本以为是灵媒右手所握的地方。

===Original===

From Boston the Allen boy went to Portland, Maine, where he succeeded “astonishingly,” till some gentleman applied the lampblack test to his assumed mediumship, whereupon he “came to grief.”
The following is copied from the *Portland Daily Press*, of March 21.

> “Exposed.﻿—The ‘wonderful’ spiritual manifestations of the ‘boy-medium,’ Master Henry B. Allen, in charge of Doctor J. H. Randall, of Boston, were brought to a sad end last evening by the impertinent curiosity and wicked doings of some of the gentlemen present at the séance at Congress Hall.
> “As usual, one of the company present was selected to sit at the side of the boy, and allowed his hand and arm to be held by both hands of the boy while the manifestations were going on. The boy seized hold of the gentleman’s wrist with his left hand, and his shoulder, or near it, with the right hand. The manifestations then began, and among them was one trick of pulling the gentleman’s hair.
> “Immediately after this trick was performed, the hand of the boy was discovered to be very black﻿—from lampblack, of the best quality, with which the gentleman had dressed his head on purpose to detect whose was the ‘spirit-hand’ that pulled his hair. His shirt sleeve, upon which the boy immediately replaced his hand after pulling his hair, was also black where the hand had been placed. The gentleman stated the facts to the company present, and the séance broke up. Dr. Randall refunded the fifty cents admission fee to those present.”

The spiritualists of the city were somewhat staggered by this exposé, but soon rallied as one of their number announced a new discovery in spiritual science. Here it is, as stated by himself:
“Whatever the electrical or ‘spirit-hand’ touches, will inevitably be transferred to the hand of the medium in every instance, unless something occurs to prevent the full operation of the law by which this result is produced. The spirit-hand being composed in part of the magnetic elements drawn from the medium, when it is dissolved again, and the magnetic fluid returns whence it came, it must of necessity carry with it whatever material substance it has touched, and leave it deposited upon the surface or material hand of the medium. This is a scientific question. How many innocent mediums have been wronged? and the invisible have permitted it, until we should discover that it was the natural result of a natural law.”
What a great discovery! and how lucidly it is set forth! The author (who, by the way, is editor of the *Portland Evening Courier*) of this new discovery, was not so modest but that he hastened to announce and claim full credit for it in the columns of the *Banner of Light*﻿—the editor of which journal congratulates him on having done so much for the cause of spiritualism! Those skeptics who were present when the lampblack was “transferred” from the gentleman’s hair to the medium’s hand, rashly concluded that the boy was an impostor. It remained for Mr. Hall﻿—that is the philosopher’s name﻿—to make the “electromagnetic transfer” discovery. The Allen boy ought ever to hold him in grateful remembrance for coming to his rescue at such a critical period, when the spirits would not vouchsafe an explanation that would exculpate him from the grievous charge of imposture. Mr. Hall deserves a leather medal now, and a soapstone monument when he is dead.

===Chinese===

离开波士顿后，艾伦男孩前往缅因州波特兰，在那里“大获成功”，直到某位绅士对他自诩的通灵术使出了“灯黑测验”，这才让他“栽了大跟头”。

以下文字摘自 3 月 21 日的《波特兰每日新闻报》（*Portland Daily Press*）：

> “骗局败露——由波士顿的 J. H. 兰德尔医生带队的‘少年灵媒’亨利·B. 艾伦少爷那套‘神奇的’亡灵显灵，昨晚在国会大厅的降神会上因在场几位绅士多管闲事的好奇心与恶劣行径而凄惨收场。
> “照例，在场众人中推选出一人坐到男孩身旁，在显灵期间任由男孩用双手握住自己的手与胳膊。男孩用左手抓住那位绅士的手腕，用右手抓住他的肩膀或靠近肩膀的位置。随后显灵开始，其中一项把戏便是扯那位绅士的头发。
> “就在这手把戏刚一演完，众人便发现男孩的那只手黑得吓人——全是上等灯黑！原来那位绅士特意在自己头上抹了灯黑，专程用来查验究竟是谁的‘幽灵之手’在扯他头发。男孩扯完头发后立刻把手放回那位绅士的衬衫袖子上，袖口沾手处也印出了一块黑印。这位绅士当场向在座众人亮明事实，降神会随之散场。兰德尔医生向到场观众退还了每人五十美分的门票费。”

城里的招魂论者起初被这桩揭秘震得有些晕头转向，但没过多久便重新振作起来，因为他们当中有一位宣布了“灵界科学”的一项新发现。以下便是他本人的原话陈述：

“凡是带电之手或‘幽灵之手’所触碰之物，除非另有外力阻挠此结果生成法则之充分运作，否则在任何情况下都必然会转移到灵媒的手上。盖因幽灵之手本就部分由灵媒身上汲取的磁性元素构成，当它再度消散、磁流体回归原处之际，必定会裹挟其所触及的任何物质微粒一并归返，并沉积在灵媒的体表或肉身手掌之上。这是一个科学问题。世上有多少无辜的灵媒曾蒙受冤屈？而无形之灵竟容忍此事发生，直到我们终于发现这原是一条自然法则的自然结果。”

多么伟大的发现！阐述得又是何等透彻明晰！这位新发现的作者（顺便一提，他正是《波特兰晚邮报》的主编）可一点儿也不谦虚，连忙在《光明旗帜报》的专栏里抢先发表并揽下全部功劳——该报主编也热忱祝贺他为招魂论事业立下了汗马功劳！当时亲眼目睹灯黑从绅士头发“转移”到灵媒手上的那些怀疑论者，竟轻率地断定这男孩是个骗子。还得靠霍尔先生（Mr. Hall）——这位大哲学家姓霍尔——方才作出了“电磁转移”的伟大发现。艾伦男孩理应永远对他怀抱感恩之心，感谢他在如此生死攸关的时刻挺身相救——毕竟当时连亡灵都不肯降下一句解释来洗刷他涉嫌欺诈的重罪。霍尔先生眼下配得上一枚牛皮奖章，死后更该立一座皂石纪念碑。

===Original===

A person, whose initials are the same as the gentleman’s named above, once lived in Aroostook, Maine, and was in the habit of attending “spiritual circles,” in which he was sometimes influenced as a “personating medium,” and to represent the symptoms of the disease which caused the controlling spirit’s translation to another sphere. It having been reported in Aroostook that a certain well-known individual, living further east, had died of cholera, a desire was expressed at the next “circle” to have him “manifest” himself. The medium above referred to got “under influence,” and personated, with an exhibition of all the symptoms of cholera, the gentleman who was reported to have died of that disease. So faithful to the supposed facts was the representation, that the medium had to be cared for as if he was himself a veritable cholera-patient. Several days after, the man who was “personated” appeared in Aroostook, alive and well, never having been attacked with the cholera. The local papers gave a graphic account of the “manifestation” soon after it occurred.
But to return to the Allen boy. After his exposure by means of the lampblack test, and Mr. Hall, of the *Portland Evening Courier*, had announced his new discovery in spiritual science, several of the Portland spiritualists had a private “sitting” with the boy. While he sat with his hands upon the arm of one of their number, they tied a rope to his wrists, and around the person’s arm, covering his hands in the way I have before described. After some wriggling and twisting (the usual amount of “nervousness,”) the bell washeard to ring behind the clotheshorse. The boy’s right hand was then examined, and it was found to be stained with some colored matter that had previously been put upon the handle of the bell. As the boy’s wrists were still tied, and the rope remained upon the man’s arm, the “transfer” theory was considered to be established as a fact, and the previous exposure shown to be not only no exposure at all, but a “stepping-stone to a grand truth in spiritual science.” Again and again did these persistent and infatuated spiritualists try what they call the “transfer test,” varying with each experiment the coloring-material used, and every time the bell was rung the medium’s right hand was found out to be stained with what had been put upon the bell-handle. By having a little slack-rope between his wrist and the man’s arm, it was not a difficult matter for the medium, while his “nervousness” was being manifested, to get hold of the bell and ring it, and to make sounds upon the strings of the dulcimer or guitar, with a drumstick that the “manager” had placed at a convenient distance from his (the boy’s) hand.
The *Portland Daily Press*, in noticing a lecture against Spiritualism, recently delivered by Dr. Von Vleck, in that city, says:﻿—“He (Dr. V. V.) performed the principal feats of the Allen boy, with his hands tied to the arm of the person with whom he was in communication.”
Horace Greeley says that if a man will be a consummate jackass and fool, he is not aware of anything in the Constitution to prevent it. I believe Mr. Greeley is right; and I think no one can reasonably be expected to exercise common sense unless he is known to possess it. It is quite natural, therefore, that many of the spiritualists, lacking common sense, should pretend to have something better.

===Chinese===

从前在缅因州阿鲁斯图克（Aroostook）住着一个人，名字首字母恰好与上面那位先生相同；他素来爱参加“降神会”，有时还会受灵力感应充当“附身模仿灵媒”，专门演绎那位主控亡灵生前死于何种疾病、借以升入另一重天界时的种种病症。有一回，阿鲁斯图克传闻更东边某个知名人物因霍乱去世，于是在接下来的一次“降神会”上，众人纷纷表示希望能请他“显灵”。上述那位灵媒立刻进入“受感状态”，活灵活现地模仿起霍乱的全部症状，扮演那位据说死于此症的绅士。他的表演逼真到几乎与假定的实情毫无二致，以至于众人不得不像照料真正的霍乱病人一样伺候他。几天之后，那位被“附身扮演”的绅士活蹦乱跳、健健康康地出现在阿鲁斯图克，压根儿就没得过霍乱。当地报纸在事发不久后便对这场“显灵”作了一番绘声绘色的报道。

言归正传，再说回艾伦男孩。自他被灯黑测验当场拆穿、而《波特兰晚邮报》的霍尔先生又宣布了灵界科学新发现之后，波特兰的几位招魂论者便与这男孩举行了一场私人“降神会谈”。男孩坐下后将双手搭在其中一人的胳膊上，众人用绳子把他的手腕和那人的胳膊绑在一起，再照我前文描述过的法子盖住他的双手。经过一番扭动挣扎（照例是那点儿“紧张抽搐”），晾衣架后面果真响起了铃铛声。众人随即查验男孩的右手，发现上面果然沾着事先涂在铃铛把手上的某种颜料。由于男孩的手腕依然被绑着，绳子也还套在那人的胳膊上，于是“转移”理论便被视作铁证如山的事实，先前的穿帮非但不再算作穿帮，反倒成了“通往灵界科学伟大真理的垫脚石”。这伙执迷不悟、死心塌地的招魂论者一遍又一遍地重复他们所谓的“转移测验”，每次实验还更换不同的着色颜料；而每次铃铛一响，灵媒的右手上总能查出铃铛把手上的同款颜料。其实只需在他的手腕与那人的胳膊之间留出一点点松绳余量，灵媒趁着“紧张发作”扭动之际，便不难伸手够到铃铛摇响它，再顺手拿起“经理”预先放在他手边顺手距离内的鼓槌，去拨弄洋琴或吉他的琴弦。

《波特兰每日新闻报》近日在报道范弗莱克医生在该市发表的一场抨击招魂论的演讲时写道：“他（范弗莱克医生）在双手被绑在通灵搭档胳膊上的情况下，当场重演了艾伦男孩的所有主要绝技。”

霍勒斯·格里利（Horace Greeley）曾说过，如果一个人执意要当个彻头彻尾的蠢驴和傻瓜，他可不晓得宪法里有什么条款能拦得住。我相信格里利先生说得对；而且我以为，若不能确定一个人本来就具备常识，也就不能指望他运用常识。因此，许多缺乏常识的招魂论者偏要自诩拥有比常识更高明的东西，也就再自然不过了。

## Part / 部

===Original===

III Trade and Business Impositions

===Chinese===

第三部 商业与贸易骗局

### XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===

Adulterations of food﻿—​Adulterations of liquor﻿—​The colonel’s whiskey﻿—​The humbugometer.
It was about eight hundred and fifty years before Christ when the young prophet cried out to his master, Elisha, over the pottage of wild gourds, “There is death in the pot!” It was two thousand six hundred and seventy years afterward, in 1820, that Accum, the chemist cried out over again, “There is death in the pot!” in the title page of a book so named, which gave almost everybody a pain in the stomach, with its horrid stories of the unhealthful humbugs sold for food and drink. This excitement has been stirred up more than once since Mr. Accum’s time, with some success; yet nothing is more certain than that a very large proportion of the food we eat, of the liquid we drink﻿—always excepting good well-filtered water﻿—and the medicines we take, not to say a word about the clothes we wear and the miscellaneous merchandise we use, is more or less adulterated with cheaper materials. Sometimes these are merely harmless; as flour, starch, annatto, lard, etc.; sometimes they are vigorous, destructive poisons﻿—as red lead, arsenic, strychnine, oil of vitriol, potash, etc.
It is not agreeable to find ourselves so thickly beset by humbugs; to find that we are not merely called on to see them, to hear them, to believe them, to invest capital in them, but to eat and drink them. Yet so it is; and, if my short discussion of this kind of humbug shall make people a little more careful, and help them to preserve their health, I shall think myself fortunate.
To begin with bread. Alum is very commonly put into it by the bakers, to make it white. Flour of inferior quality, “runny” flour, and even that from wormy wheat﻿—ground-up worms, bugs, and all﻿—is often mixed in as much as the case will bear. Potato flour has been known to be mixed with wheat; and so, thirty years ago, were plaster-of-Paris, bone-dust, white clay, etc. But these are little used now, if at all; and the worst thing in bread, aside from bad flour, which is bad enough, is usually the alum. It is often put in ready mixed with salt, and it accomplishes two things, viz., to make the bread white, and to suck up a good deal of water, and make the bread weigh well. It has been sometimes found that the alum was put in at the mill instead of the bakery.
Milk is most commonly adulterated with cold water; and many are the jokes on the milkmen about their best cow being choked etc., by a turnip in the pump-spout﻿—their “cow with the wooden tail” (i.e., the pump-handle,) and so on. Awful stories are told about the London milkmen, who are said to manufacture a fearful kind of medicine to be sold as milk, the cream being made of a quantity of calf’s brain beaten to a slime. Stories are told around New York, too, of a mysterious powder sold by druggists, which with water makes milk; but it is milk that must be used quickly, or it turns into a curious mess. But the worst adulteration of milk is to adulterate the old cow herself; as is done in the swill-milk establishments which received such an exposure a few years ago in a city paper. This milk is still furnished; and many a poor little baby is daily suffering convulsions from its effects. So difficult is it to find real milk for babies in the city, that physicians often prescribe the use of what is called “condensed” milk instead; which, though very different from milk not evaporated, is at least made of the genuine article. A series of careful experiments to develop the milk-humbug was made by a competent physician in Boston within a few years, but he found the milk there (aside from swill-milk) adulterated with nothing worse than water, salt, and burnt sugar.

===Chinese===

食品掺假——酒类掺假——上校的威士忌——欺罔测度仪。

大约在公元前八百五十年，一位年轻的先知对着一锅野瓜羹向他的师父以利沙（Elisha）高呼：“锅里有致死之物！”两千六百七十年后，到了 1820 年，化学家阿库姆（Accum）在一部同名著作的扉页上再度呼喊：“锅里有致死之物！”书中所载那些当作饮食出售的有毒骗局（humbug）故事骇人听闻，几乎令每位读者读后胃里一阵翻江倒海。自阿库姆先生的时代以来，类似的舆论风波已不止一次被掀起，且收效颇丰；然而，有一点再确定不过：我们吃进嘴里的食物、喝进肚里的饮品（上好且充分过滤的清水除外）以及吞服的药物——更不必提身上穿的衣裳与日常使用的杂项商品——其中相当大一部分，都或多或少掺杂了更为廉价的物料。有时这些掺杂物尚算无害，比如面粉、淀粉、胭脂树橙、猪油之类；有时却是猛烈致命的剧毒，譬如红丹、砒霜、马钱子碱、硫酸、苛性钾等等。

发觉自己竟被欺罔（humbug）如此密不透风地团团围住，绝非什么愉快的体验；更糟糕的是，我们不光被要求去观看它们、倾听它们、轻信它们、往里头投钱，甚至还得把它们吃进肚子里、喝进肠胃中。可现实偏偏如此；倘若我对这类欺罔的简短探讨能令世人多长个心眼，帮大家守住健康，我便深感荣幸了。

先从面包说起。面包师极常往面包里掺入明矾，好让面色雪白。劣质面粉、“稀软”面粉，甚至生虫小麦磨出的面粉——连虫带蛾一并磨碎——也常常按面团所能承受的极限大量混入。马铃薯粉也素有混入小麦粉的先例；三十年前，人们甚至还往里掺熟石膏、骨粉、白陶土等等。如今这些东西即使还有人用，也已寥寥无几；眼下面包里最糟糕的成分，除了本身已够劣质的坏面粉之外，通常便是明矾。它常与食盐预先混好加入面中，一举两得：既能使面包洁白，又能吸足大量水分，让面包压秤足斤。有时甚至查出明矾根本不是在面包房加的，而是在磨坊里就掺进去了。

牛奶最常见的掺假物是冷水；世人对送奶工打趣的笑话多得数不清，譬如他们最好的奶牛被一根卡在抽水机嘴里的萝卜给噎住了、他们养的是“长着木尾巴的奶牛”（指抽水机压杆）等等。关于伦敦送奶工的传说更是骇人听闻，据说他们能配制出一种可怕的药水充当牛奶出售，上面的奶油竟是用大量小牛脑髓捣成黏糊做成的。纽约一带也流传着药店出售一种神秘粉末的说法，兑上水就能变出牛奶；只不过这种牛奶必须赶紧喝掉，否则很快便会变成一摊古怪的糊糊。然而牛奶最恶劣的掺假，莫过于直接把老母牛本身“掺假”——就像几年前某家都市报大力曝光过的那些“酒糟奶”养殖场干的好事。这种奶至今仍在供应；不知多少可怜的小婴儿每天都在因此浑身抽搐。在城里要想替婴儿寻得真牛奶实在太难，以至于医生常常改开所谓的“炼乳”；炼乳虽与未经蒸发的鲜奶大相径庭，至少还是拿真货制成的。近几年波士顿有位称职的医生做过一系列严谨实验以揭穿牛奶骗局，结果发觉当地牛奶（除酒糟奶外）掺的最坏的东西也不过是水、盐和焦糖罢了。

===Original===

Tea is bejuggled first by John Chinaman, who is a very cunning rascal; and second, by the seller here. Green and black tea are made from the same plant, but by different processes﻿—the green being most expensive. To meet the increased demand for green tea, Master John takes immense quantities of black tea and “paints” it, by stirring into it over a fire a fine powder of plaster Paris and Prussian-blue, at the rate of half a pound to each hundred pounds of tea. John also sometimes takes a very cheap kind, and puts on a nice gloss by stirring it in gum-water, with some stove-polish in it. We may imagine ourselves, after drinking this kind of tea, with a beautiful black gloss on our insides. John moreover, manufactures vast quantities of what he plainly calls “Lie-tea.” This is dust and refuse of tea-leaves and other leaves, made up with dust and starch or gum into little lumps, and used to adulterate better tea. Seven hundred and fifty thousand pounds of this nice stuff were imported into England in one period of eighteen months. It seems to be used in New-York only for green tea.
Coffee is adulterated with chicory-root (which costs only about one-third as much)﻿—dandelion-root, peas, beans, mangold-wurzel, wheat, rye, acorns, carrots, parsnips, horse-chestnuts, and sometimes with livers of horses and cattle! All these things are roasted or baked to the proper color and consistency, and then mixed in. No great sympathy need be expended on those who suffer from this particular humbug, however; for when it is so easy to buy the real berry, and roast or at least grind it one’s self, it is our own fault if our laziness leaves us to eat all those sorts of stuff.
Cocoa is “extended” with sugar, starch, flour, iron-rust, Venetian-red, grease, and various earths. But it is believed by pretty good authority that the American-made preparations of cocoa are nearly or quite pure. Even if they are not the whole bean can be used instead.
Butter and lard have one tenth, and sometimes even one-quarter, of water mixed up in them. It is easy to find this out by melting a sample before the fire and putting it away to cool, when the humbug appears by the grease going up, and the water, perhaps turbid with whey, settling below.
Honey is humbugged with sugar or molasses. Sugar is not often sanded as the old stories have it. Fine white sugar is sometimes floured pretty well; and brown sugar is sometimes made of a portion of good sugar with a cheaper kind mixed in. Inferior brown sugars are often full of a certain crab-like animalcule or minute bug, often visible without a microscope, in water where the sugar is dissolved. It is believed that this pleasing insect sometimes gets into the skin, and produces a kind of itch. I do not believe there is much danger of adulteration in good loaf or crushed white sugar, or good granulated or brown sugar.

===Chinese===

茶叶的捣鬼，头一道出自极其狡猾的华商“约翰佬”（John Chinaman）之手；第二道则出自本地茶商。绿茶与红茶本采自同一种茶树，只是制法各异——绿茶价格最贵。为了应付日益增长的绿茶需求，约翰老板便取来大宗红茶给它“上色”，做法是在火上一面烘炒，一面往里撒入石膏粉与普鲁士蓝研磨的细末，每百磅茶叶掺半磅。有时约翰佬也会拿极廉价的茶末，拌入掺了炉膛黑铅粉的胶水里翻炒，使其表面泛出漂亮的油亮光泽。大伙儿不妨想象一下，喝完这种茶之后，咱们的五脏六腑想必也会泛起一层迷人的乌黑亮泽。更有甚者，约翰佬还大批量制造一种他自己直截了当称之为“假茶”（Lie-tea）的货色。这是用茶叶末、其他树叶碎渣，拌上尘土和淀粉或树胶捏成的小颗粒，专门用来掺进上等茶叶里。在短短十八个月内，英格兰就进口了七十五万磅这种“妙物”。而在纽约，它似乎只被用来掺进绿茶。

咖啡里掺的东西包括菊苣根（价钱只有咖啡的三分之一左右）、蒲公英根、豌豆、菜豆、饲用甜菜、小麦、黑麦、橡子、胡萝卜、防风草、七叶树果，有时甚至还有马肝和牛肝！所有这些杂物都烘焙到恰当的色泽与质地，然后混入咖啡粉中。不过，对中了这招骗局的人倒也大可不必太同情；既然买到整颗真咖啡豆、自己烘焙或至少自己研磨如此省事，若因懒惰而把上述种种杂物吞进肚里，那纯粹是咎由自取。

可可粉则靠糖、淀粉、面粉、铁锈、威尼斯红、油脂和各类泥土来“增量”。不过据相当可靠的权威人士认为，美国本土生产的可可制品几乎或完全是纯净的。即便不是，也可以直接买整颗可可豆代替。

黄油和猪油里常搅进十分之一、有时甚至四分之一的水分。要想查明这一点再简单不过：只需取一小块样品在火前融化，再搁置冷却，欺罔便会原形毕露——油脂浮在上面，水分（兴许还混着乳清显得浑浊）则沉在底下。

蜂蜜里掺的是砂糖或糖蜜。砂糖倒不像老故事里常说的那样经常掺沙子。精制白糖有时会狠狠掺进一通面粉；红糖则有时是用一部分好糖掺进廉价劣糖混制而成。劣质红糖里往往满是一种像小螃蟹似的微小虫豸，把糖化在水里时，不用显微镜常能直接用肉眼看见。据说这种可爱的小虫有时还会钻进皮肤里，引起一种疥疮。不过我相信，上等块糖、碎白糖、优质砂糖或好红糖里，掺假的风险并不算大。

===Original===

Pepper is mixed with fine dust, dirt, linseed-meal, ground rice, or mustard and wheat-flour; ginger, with wheat flour colored by turmeric and reinforced by cayenne. Cinnamon is sometimes not present at all in what is so called﻿—the stuff being the inferior and cheaper cassia bark; sometimes it is only part cassia; sometimes the humbug part of it is flour and ochre. Cayenne-pepper is mixed with cornmeal and salt, Venetian-red, mustard, brickdust, fine sawdust, and red-lead. Mustard with flour and turmeric. Confectionery is often poisoned with Prussian-blue, Antwerp-blue, gamboge, ultramarine, chrome yellow, red-lead, white-lead, vermilion, Brunswick-green, and Scheele’s green, or arsenite of copper! Never buy any confectionery that is colored or painted. Vinegar is made of whisky, or of oil of vitriol. Pickles have verdigris in them to make them a pretty green. “Pretty green” he must be who will eat bought pickles! Preserved fruits often have verdigris in them, too.
An awful list! Imagine a meal of such bewitched food, where the actual articles are named. “Take some of the alum bread.” “Have a cup of pea-soup and chicory-coffee?” “I’ll trouble you for the oil-of-vitriol, if you please.” “Have some sawdust on your meat, or do you prefer this flour and turmeric mustard?” “A piece of this verdigris-preserve gooseberry pie, Madam?” “Won’t you put a few more sugar-bugs in your ash-leaf tea?” “Do you prefer black tea, or Prussian-blue tea?” “Do you like your tea with swill-milk, or without?”
I have not left myself space to speak of the tricks played by the druggists and the liquor-dealers; but I propose to devote another chapter exclusively to the adulteration of liquors in this country. It is a subject so fearful and so important that nothing less than a chapter can do it justice. I must now end with a story or two and a suggestion or two.
Old Colonel P. sold much whisky; and his manner was to sell by sample out of a pure barrel overnight, at a marvelous cheap rate, and then to “rectify” before morning, under pretence of coopering and marking. Certain persons having a grudge against the Colonel, once made an arrangement with a carman, who executed their plan, thus:﻿—He went to the Colonel, and asked to see whisky. The jolly old fellow took him downstairs and showed him a great cellar full. Carman samples a barrel. “Fust rate, Colonel, how d’ye sell it?” Colonel names his price on the rectified basis. “Well, Colonel, how much yer got?” “So many barrels﻿—two or three hundred.” “Colonel, here’s your money. I’ll take the lot.” “All right,” says Colonel P.; “there’s some coopering to be done on it; some of the hoops and heads are a very little loose. You shall have it all in the morning.” “No, colonel, we’ll roll it right out this minnit! My trucks are up there, all ready.” And, sure enough, he had a string of a dozen or more brigaded in the street. The Colonel was sadly dumbfounded; he turned several colors﻿—red mostly﻿—stammered, made excuses. It was no go, the whisky was the customer’s, and the game was up. The humbugged old humbug finally “came down,” and bought his man off by paying him several hundred dollars.

===Chinese===

胡椒粉里混着细尘、泥土、亚麻籽粉、米粉，或者芥末粉与小麦粉；姜粉里混着拿姜黄染过色、又加了辣椒提味的小麦粉。所谓肉桂粉里有时压根儿半点肉桂都没有——装的竟是劣质廉价的桂皮；有时只掺一部分桂皮；有时掺假的成分则是面粉和赭石。辣椒粉里混着玉米粉、食盐、威尼斯红、芥末、砖末、细锯末乃至红丹。芥末粉里混着面粉和姜黄。糖果糕点里则常常被下毒，掺入普鲁士蓝、安特卫普蓝、藤黄、群青、铬黄、红丹、铅白、朱砂、不伦瑞克绿以及舍勒绿（即砷酸铜）！凡是染过色或涂了颜料的糖果，千万别买。醋是用威士忌或硫酸勾兑的。腌菜里掺了铜绿，好叫它们显得“嫩绿水灵”。肯吃买来的腌菜的人，脑子怕是也够“嫩绿水灵（生瓜蛋子）”的！蜜饯水果里也常常掺有铜绿。

真是触目惊心的一张清单！试想一下，摆上一桌如此施了魔法的饭菜，且直呼其真名实姓，该是何等光景：“来点儿明矾面包吧。”“要喝杯豌豆汤配菊苣咖啡吗？”“劳驾，请把硫酸递给我好吗？”“您肉上撒点儿锯末，还是更喜欢这面粉姜黄芥末酱？”“夫人，来块这铜绿蜜饯醋栗派吗？”“您的白蜡树叶茶里要不要再多放几只糖虫子？”“您喜欢喝红茶，还是普鲁士蓝茶？”“您喝茶要兑酒糟奶，还是不兑？”

我已没有篇幅再谈药剂师和酒商玩的那些鬼把戏了；但我打算另辟一章，专门探讨本国酒类的掺假。这个话题既骇人听闻又至关重要，非得整整一章方能说透。眼下，我只得以一两则故事和一两条建议收尾。

老 P. 上校卖过不少威士忌；他的惯用伎俩是头天晚上从一桶纯正原浆里舀出样品给买家品尝，报出奇低的价格，然后借口要修桶、刷唛头，赶在天亮前把酒“勾兑掺水”。有几个与上校结了怨的人，有一回买通了一名马车夫，照此计策行事：车夫找上门去，说要看威士忌。那快活的老家伙领他下楼，给他看了一整地窖满满当当的酒桶。车夫从桶里舀了一口尝尝。“顶呱呱啊，上校，这酒怎么卖？”上校按勾兑后的成本价报出了价钱。“好咧，上校，您一共有多少桶？”“这么多桶——两三百桶吧。”“上校，给您现钱。这批货我全包了。”“好说，”P. 上校说道，“不过还得修整一下酒桶；有几只桶箍和桶盖稍微有点儿松。明儿一早全给您备齐。”“不劳烦，上校，咱们这就一分钟也不耽搁，立马滚出去装车！我的货车全在街上候着呢。”果不其然，街上一字排开停着一打多辆大车。上校当场傻了眼；脸色一阵青一阵白——多数时候涨得通红——结结巴巴地找起借口。可没用，酒钱已付，货归买主，戏法演砸了。这个反被糊弄的老骗子最终只得“认栽掏腰包”，赔了那人好几百美元才把这桩买卖买断脱身。

===Original===

There is a much older and better known story about a grocer who was a deacon, and who was heard to call downstairs before breakfast, to his clerk: “John, have you watered the rum?” “Yes, Sir.” “And sanded the sugar?” “Yes, Sir.” “And dusted the pepper?” “Yes, Sir.” “And chicoried the coffee?” “Yes, Sir.” “Then come up to prayers.” Let us hope that the grocers of the present day, while they adulterate less, do not pray less.
Between 1851 and 1854, Mr. Wakley of the *London Lancet* gave an awful roasting to the adulteration-interest in London. He employed an able analyzer, who began by going about without telling what he was at; and buying a great number of samples of all kinds of food, drugs, etc., at a great number of shops. Then he analyzed them; and when he found humbug in any sample, he published the facts, and the seller’s name and place of business. It may be imagined what a terrible row this kicked up. Very numerous and violent threats were made; but the *Lancet*, was never once sued by any of the aggrieved, for it had told the truth.
Perhaps some discouraged reader may ask, What can I eat? Well, I don’t pretend to direct people’s diet. Ask your doctor, if you can’t find out. But I will suggest that there are a few things that can’t be adulterated. You can’t adulterate an egg, nor an oyster, nor an apple, nor a potato, nor a salt codfish; and if they are spoiled they will notify you themselves! and when good, they are all good healthy food. In short, one good safeguard is, to use, as far as you can, things with their life in them when you buy them, whether vegetable or animal. The next best rule against these adulteration-humbugs is, to buy goods crude instead of manufactured; coffee, and pepper, and spices, etc., whole instead of ground, for instance. Thus, though you give more work, you buy purity with it. And lastly, there are various chemical processes, and the microscope, to detect adulterations; and milk, in particular, may always be tested by a lactometer﻿—a simple little instrument which the milkmen use, which costs a few shillings, and which tells the story in an instant. It is a glass bulb, with a stem above and a scale on it, and a weight below. In good average milk, at sixty degrees of heat, the lactometer floats at twenty on its scale; and in poorer milk, at from that figure down. If it floats at fifteen, the milk is one-fourth water; if at ten, one half.
It would be a wonderful thing for mankind if some philosophic Yankee would contrive some kind of “ometer” that would measure the infusion of humbug in anything. A “Humbugometer” he might call it. I would warrant him a good sale.

===Chinese===

还有一个更古老、更脍炙人口的故事，说的是一位身兼教会执事的杂货商，早饭前被人听见冲着楼下店员喊道：“约翰，朗姆酒兑水了没有？”“兑好了，先生。”“糖里掺沙子了没有？”“掺好了，先生。”“胡椒粉里掺尘土了没有？”“掺好了，先生。”“咖啡里掺菊苣了没有？”“掺好了，先生。”“那好，上来做早祷吧。”但愿当今的杂货商们少掺点假，祷告可别跟着少了。

1851 年至 1854 年间，《伦敦柳叶刀》（*London Lancet*）的韦克利先生（Mr. Wakley）把伦敦的造假利益集团狠狠炙烤了一番。他聘请了一位精明的化验师，起初不动声色地四处走动，在大量店铺里采购了各式各样的食品、药品样品。随后他逐一化验；只要在任何样品里查出欺罔掺假，便立即公布事实真相，连带卖家的姓名与营业地址一并曝光。可想而知这掀起了多大的轩然大波。无数人发出暴跳如雷的威胁；然而《柳叶刀》自始至终从未被任何一位自称受损的商家告上法庭，只因它说的句句是实话。

或许有泄气的读者会问：那我还能吃什么呢？好吧，我不敢妄自指点诸位的日常饮食。若是拿不准，去问你们的医生。不过我倒可以提个醒，世上有几样东西是没法掺假的：你没法往鸡蛋里掺假，没法往牡蛎、苹果、土豆或咸鳕鱼里掺假；而且它们要是坏了，自己就会发出警报！新鲜的时候，又全都是极佳的健康食物。总而言之，一条好用的防身准则，便是买菜买肉时尽量挑选还带着“生机”的整物，无论植物还是动物皆然。防范掺假骗局的第二条良策，是买未经加工的原料而非成品；譬如咖啡、胡椒、香料等等，买整粒的而不是磨成粉的。如此一来，你虽多费了些工夫，却换来了货真价实。最后，还有各种化学检验法和显微镜可以查出掺假；尤其是牛奶，随时都可以用乳汁比重计（lactometer）来测——那是送奶工自己用的简易小仪器，只需几先令，瞬间便能见分晓。它是一个玻璃球泡，上头带一根刻有分度的细管，底端缀着配重。在华氏六十度的水温下，放进中等品质的好牛奶里，乳汁比重计会浮在刻度二十的位置；品质越差，读数就越往下掉。要是浮在十五，牛奶里就掺了四分之一的水；要是浮在十，就掺了一半的水。

倘若哪位富于哲思的扬基奇才真能发明某种“测度仪”，用来精确测定任何事物里掺了多少欺罔成分，那可真是造福人类的伟大发明。他大可称之为“欺罔测度仪”（Humbugometer）。我敢包管他这买卖一定大卖。

### XIX / 第十九章

===Original===

Adulterations in drinks﻿—​Riding home on your wine-barrel﻿—​List of things to make rum﻿—​Things to color it with﻿—​Canal-boat hash﻿—​English adulteration law﻿—​Effects of drugs used﻿—​How to use them﻿—​Buying liquors under the customhouse lock﻿—​A homeoopathic dose.
As long as the people of the United States tipple down rum and other liquors at the rate of a good deal more than one hundred million gallons a year, besides what is imported and what is called imported﻿—as long as they pay for their tippling a good deal more than fifty millions, and probably over a hundred millions of dollars a year﻿—so long it will be a great object to manufacture false liquors, and sell them at the price of true ones. When liquor of good quality costs from four to fifteen dollars a gallon, and an imitation can be had that tastes just as good, and has just as much “jizm” in it﻿—and probably a good deal more﻿—for from twenty-five cents to one dollar a gallon, somebody will surely make and sell that imitation.
Adulterating and imitating liquors is a very large business; and I don’t know of anybody who will deny that this particular humbug is very extensively cultivated. There are a great many people, however, who will talk about it as they do in Western towns about fever and ague: “We don’t do anything of the kind here, but those other people over there do!”
There is very little pure liquor, either malt or spirituous, to be obtained in any way. The more you pay for it, as a rule, the more the publican gains, but what you drink is none the purer. Importing don’t help you. Port is﻿—or used to be, for very little is now made, comparatively﻿—imitated in immense quantities at Oporto; and in the logwood trade, the European winemakers competed with the dyers. It is a London proverb, that if you want genuine port-wine, you have got to go to Oporto and make your own wine, and then ride on the barrel all the way home. It is perhaps possible to get pure wine in France by buying it at the vineyard; but if any dealer has had it, give up the idea!
As for what is done this side of the water, now for it. I do not rely upon the old work of Mr. “Death-in-the-pot Accum,” printed some thirty years ago, in England. My statements come mostly from a New York book put forth within a few years by a New York man, whose name is now in the Directory, and whose business is said to consist to a great extent in furnishing one kind or another of the queer stuff he talks about, to brewers, or distillers, or wine and brandy merchants.

===Chinese===

饮品掺假——骑在自家酒桶上回家——配制朗姆酒的原料清单——给酒上色的原料——运河客船的杂烩——英国的防掺假法——所用毒药的药效——如何使用这些毒药——在海关封条下买酒——一剂顺势疗法微剂量。

只要美国民众每年灌进肚里的朗姆酒和其他烈酒远远超过一亿加仑——这还不算真正进口的和号称进口的——只要他们每年花在买醉上的钱远远超过五千万、恐怕还得有一亿多美元，那么炮制假酒并按真酒的价钱兜售，就永远是一桩大有油水可图的营生。当上等好酒每加仑要价四至十五美元，而只需花二十五美分到一美元一加仑就能弄出味道一般无二、甚至“劲道”丝毫不差——多半还更冲——的仿制品时，世上自然少不了有人去造假贩假。

酒类掺假与仿造是一门规模庞大无比的生意；我想任谁也否认不了，这门特殊的欺罔（humbug）行当如今正被大肆耕耘。不过，世上有许多人在谈及此事时，活像西部市镇居民聊起疟疾寒热病那样：“我们这儿可从不干这种勾当，是那头那帮人干的！”

无论是麦芽酒还是烈性蒸馏酒，如今无论通过什么门路都极难买到真正的纯酒。照规矩，你付的价钱越高，酒馆老板赚得越狠，可你喝进嘴里的酒却丝毫不会更纯。指望进口货也救不了你。波特酒（Port）过去——或者说曾经，因为如今真货相对酿得极少——就在波尔图当地被人成批仿造；在苏木（logwood）贸易中，欧洲酿酒商甚至跟染坊抢货源。伦敦有句谚语：你要是想喝货真价实的波特酒，就得亲自去一趟波尔图亲手酿酒，然后一路骑在酒桶上护送回家。在法国，兴许直接去葡萄园买酒还能买到纯正葡萄酒；但只要经过任何酒商的手，趁早死了这条心吧！

至于大西洋这一岸都在干些什么勾当，咱们这就来细数。我并不依赖那位人称“锅中有毒的阿库姆”先生三十年前在英格兰印行的老书。我的陈述主要出自一位纽约人近几年出版的一本纽约本地著作——此人的名字至今还印在纽约工商名录里，据说他的主业在很大程度上就是向啤酒厂、蒸馏酒厂或葡萄酒与白兰地酒商供应他书里所谈的种种稀奇古怪的配料。

===Original===

This gentleman, in a sweet alphabetical miscellany of drugs, herbs, minerals, and groceries commonly used in manufacturing our best Old Bourbon whisky, Swan gin, Madeira wine, pale ale, London brown stout, Heidsieck, Clicquot, Lafitte, and other nice drinks; names the chief of such ingredients as follows:
Aloes, alum, calamus (flag-root) capsicum, *cocculus indicus*, copperas, coriander-seed, gentian-root, ginger, grains-of-paradise, honey, liquorice, logwood, molasses, onions, opium, orange-peel, quassia, salt, stramonium-seed (deadly nightshade), sugar of lead, sulphite of soda, sulphuric acid, tobacco, turpentine, vitriol, yarrow. I have left strychnine out of the list, as some persons have doubts about this poison ever being used in adulterating liquors. A wholesale liquor-dealer in New York city, however, assures me that more than one-half the so-called whisky is poisoned with it.
Besides these twenty-seven kinds of rum, here come twenty-three more articles, used to put the right color to it when it is made; by making a soup of one or another, and stirring it in at the right time. I alphabet these, too: alkanet-root, annatto, barwood, blackberry, blue-vitriol, brazil-wood, burnt sugar, cochineal, elderberry, garancine (an extract of madder), indigo, Nicaragua-wood, orchil, pokeberry, potash, quercitron, red beet, red cabbage, red carrots, saffron, sanders-wood, turmeric, whortleberry.
In all, in both lists, just fifty. There are more, however. But that’s enough. Now then, my friend, what did you drink this morning? You called it Bourbon, or Cognac, or Old Otard, very likely, but what was it? The “glorious uncertainty” of drinking liquor under these circumstances is enough to make a man’s head swim without his getting drunk at all. There might, perhaps, be found a consolation like that of the Western traveller about the hash. “When I travel in a canal-boat or steamboat,” quoth this brave and stout-stomached man, “I always eat the hash, because then I know what I’ve got!”
It was a good many years ago that the Parliament of England found it necessary to make a law to prevent sophisticating malt liquors. Here is the list of things they forbid to put into beer: “molasses, honey, liquorice, vitriol, quassia, *cocculus indicus*, grains-of-paradise, Guinea-pepper, opium.” The penalty was one thousand dollars fine on the brewer, and two thousand five hundred dollars on the druggist who supplied him.

===Chinese===

这位先生在他那份按字母表顺序排列、琳琅满目的药材、草药、矿物与杂货清单里——据称是日常用来勾兑我们最好的老波旁威士忌、天鹅牌金酒、马德拉葡萄酒、淡色艾尔啤酒、伦敦黑烈性啤酒、白雪香槟（Heidsieck）、凯歌香槟（Clicquot）、拉菲葡萄酒（Lafitte）及其他美酒的常用料——列出了以下主要成分：

芦荟、明矾、菖蒲根、辣椒、*印度防己子*（*cocculus indicus*）、硫酸亚铁（绿矾）、芫荽籽、龙胆根、生姜、天堂椒、蜂蜜、甘草、苏木、糖蜜、洋葱、鸦片、橙皮、苦木、食盐、曼陀罗籽（颠茄类剧毒植物）、醋酸铅（铅糖）、亚硫酸钠、硫酸、烟草、松节油、硫酸铜/硫酸液、蓍草。我还没把马钱子碱（士的宁）放进这张清单，因为有些人怀疑这种剧毒是否真被用于酒类掺假。然而，纽约市一位批发酒商向我信誓旦旦地保证，市面上所谓威士忌的一半以上都掺了这味毒药。

除了这二十七种配制朗姆酒的原料，下面还有二十三种物品，专门用来在酒配好后调制出以假乱真的色泽——挑其中一两种熬成浓汤，在恰当时机搅入酒中。我也把它们按字母序开列如下：紫草根、胭脂树橙、紫檀红木、黑莓、胆矾（硫酸铜）、巴西苏木、焦糖、胭脂虫红、接骨木果、茜草红（茜草提取物）、靛蓝、尼加拉瓜红木、苔藓紫、美洲商陆果、苛性钾、槲皮黄、红甜菜、紫甘蓝、红胡萝卜、藏红花、檀香红木、姜黄、越橘。

两张清单合计，整整五十种。其实远不止这些。不过这也够瞧的了。好了，我的朋友，你今早喝的究竟是什么？你嘴上管它叫波旁威士忌、干邑白兰地，或者老奥塔德（Old Otard），可它到底是个什么玩意儿？在这种境况下喝酒那种“奇妙的不确定感”，简直不用喝醉就足以叫人天旋地转。或许人们只能像那位西部旅客谈论杂烩肉丁一样自我宽慰了。“每当我坐运河船或蒸汽船出门旅行，”这位胆大胃壮的好汉如是说，“我向来只吃杂烩肉丁，因为至少那样我心里有数，知道自己吃进去的是什么玩意儿！”

早在许多年前，英国议会便发觉有必要立法禁止在麦芽酒中掺假作伪。以下便是法律明令禁止加入啤酒的物品清单：“糖蜜、蜂蜜、甘草、硫酸、苦木、*印度防己子*、天堂椒、几内亚胡椒、鸦片。”违者罚款重惩：酿酒商罚一千美元，向其供货的药商罚两千五百美元。

===Original===

I know of no such law in this country. The theory of our government leaves people to take care of themselves as much as possible. But now let us see what some of these fifty ingredients will do. Beets and carrots, honey and liquorice, orange-peel and molasses, will not do much harm; though I should think tipplers would prefer them as the customer at the eating-house preferred his flies, “on a separate plate.” But the case is different with *cocculus indicus*, and stramonium, and sulphuric acid, and sugar of lead, and the like. I take the following accounts, so far as they are medical, from a standard work by Dr. Dunglison:﻿—Aloes is a cathartic. Cocculus indicus contains picrotoxin, which is an “acrid narcotic poison;” from five to ten grains will kill a strong dog. The boys often call it “cockle-cinders;” they pound it and mix it in dough, and throw it into the water to catch fish. The poor fish eat it, soon become delirious, whirling and dancing furiously about on the top of the water, and then die. Copperas tends to produce nausea, vomiting, griping, and purging. Grains-of-paradise, a large kind of cardamom, is “strongly heating and carminative” (i.e., anti-flatulent and antispasmodic.) Opium is known well enough. Stramonium-seed would seem to have been made on purpose for the liquor business. In moderate doses it is a powerful narcotic, producing vertigo, headache, dimness or perversion of vision (i.e., seeing double) and confusion of thought. (N.B. What else does liquor do?) In larger doses (still like liquor,) you obtain these symptoms aggravated; and then a delirium, sometimes whimsical (snakes in your boots) and sometimes furious, a stupor, convulsions, and death. A fine drink this stramonium? Sugar of lead is what is called a cumulative poison; having the quality of remaining in the system when taken in small quantities, and piling itself up, as it were, until there is enough to accomplish something, when it causes debility, paralysis, and other things. Sulphuric acid is strongly corrosive﻿—a powerful caustic, attacking the teeth, even when very dilute; eating up flesh and bones alike when strong enough; and, if taken in a large enough dose, an awfully tearing and agonizing fatal poison.
The way to use these delectable nutriments is in part as follows:﻿—Stir a little sulphuric acid into your beer. This will give you a fine “old ale” in about a quarter of a minute. Take a mixture of alum, salt, and copperas, ground fine, and stir into your beer, and this will make it froth handsomely. Cocculus indicus, tobacco-leaves, and stramonium, cooked in the beer, etc., give it force. Potash is sometimes stirred into wine to correct acidity. Sulphite of soda is now very commonly stirred into cider, to keep it from fermenting further. Sugar of lead is stirred into wines to make them clear, and to keep them sweet. And so on, through the whole long list.
It is a curious instance of people’s quiet acknowledgment of their own foolishness, that a popular form of the invitation to take a drink is, “Come and h’ist in some pizen!”

===Chinese===

我在本国却没听说过有这等法律。我们政府的治国理念是尽可能让老百姓自己照管自己。不过，现在让我们瞧瞧这五十种配料里有几样到底会干出什么好事。甜菜、胡萝卜、蜂蜜、甘草、橙皮和糖蜜倒不至于害人性命；不过我猜酒鬼们大概更宁愿像小饭馆里的顾客要求把苍蝇“另盛一盘”那样，把这些东西单独端上来。但*印度防己子*、曼陀罗、硫酸和铅糖之流可就大不相同了。以下医学药理说明，均摘自邓利森医生（Dr. Dunglison）的一部权威著作：芦荟是烈性泻药。印度防己子含有印防己毒素，是一种“辛辣的麻醉性剧毒”；五到十格令便足以毒死一条壮狗。顽童们常管它叫“毒鱼籽”，把它捣碎拌进面团扔进水里药鱼。可怜的鱼儿吃下去后很快神志不清，在水面上疯狂打转乱舞，随即毙命。硫酸亚铁易引发恶心、呕吐、腹绞痛与剧烈腹泻。天堂椒是一种大粒豆蔻，“药性极燥热且具驱风止痉之效”。鸦片则人人皆知。曼陀罗籽简直像是专程为酒业造出来的：中等剂量便是强效麻醉剂，引发眩晕、头痛、视力模糊或错乱（即视物重影）以及思维混乱。（注：除此之外烈酒还能让人怎样呢？）剂量再大些（依然和烈酒如出一辙），上述症状便会加剧；继而出现谵妄——时而产生奇异幻觉（觉得靴子里爬满了蛇），时而狂躁暴怒——随后陷入昏迷、抽搐乃至死亡。这曼陀罗倒真是杯佳酿啊？醋酸铅（铅糖）属于所谓“蓄积性毒物”；小剂量摄入时会滞留体内、层层堆积，直至蓄积到足够分量，便会引发全身衰弱、瘫痪及其他重疾。硫酸则具极强腐蚀性——一种猛烈的苛性酸，即便高度稀释也会侵蚀牙齿；浓度够高时连皮带骨一并啃噬殆尽；倘若服下足够剂量，更是撕心裂肺、痛不欲生的致命毒药。

这些“美味滋补品”的用法大致如下：往啤酒里搅进一点硫酸，不出十五秒钟，便能造出一杯上好的“陈年艾尔啤酒”。把明矾、食盐与硫酸亚铁磨成细粉搅进啤酒，酒沫立刻翻涌得漂亮极了。把印度防己子、烟叶与曼陀罗放进啤酒里一煮，便平添几分劲道。葡萄酒发酸时，往里搅些苛性钾便能中和。如今苹果酒里也极常搅入亚硫酸钠，防止其继续发酵。铅糖则被搅入葡萄酒中，用以澄清酒液并保持甜味。如此这般，长长一整张清单莫不各有妙用。

人们私下里其实也默认自己的蠢行，从一句流行的邀酒俗话便可见一斑：“来，干了这杯毒药！”

===Original===

I know of no plan by which anybody can be sure of obtaining pure liquor of any description. Some persons always purchase their wines and liquors while they are under the customhouse lock and consequently before they have reached the hands of the importer. Yet there are scores of men in New York and Philadelphia who have made large fortunes by sending whisky to France, there refining, coloring, flavoring, and doctoring it, then re-shipping it to New York as French brandy, paying the duty, and selling it before it has left the customhouse! There is a locality in France where a certain brand of wine is made. It is adulterated with red-lead, and every year more or less of the inhabitants of that locality are attacked with “lead-colic,” caused by drinking this poisoned wine right at the fountainhead where it is made. There is more bogus champagne drank in any one year, in the city of Paris alone, than there is genuine champagne made in any one year in the world. America ordinarily consumes more so-called champagne annually than is made in the world, and yet nearly all the genuine champagne in the world is taken by the courts of Europe. The genuine Hock wine made at Johannisberg on the Rhine is worth three dollars per bottle by the large quantity, and nearly all of it is shipped to Russia; yet, at any of the hotels in the village of Johannisberg, within half a mile from the winepresses of the pure article, you can be supplied for a dollar per bottle with what purports to be the genuine Hock wine. Since chemistry has enabled liquor dealers to manufacture any description of wine or liquor for twenty-five cents to a dollar a gallon, there are annually made and sold thousands of gallons of wine and brandy that never smelt a grape.
Suppose a wholesale liquor-merchant imports genuine brandy. He usually “rectifies” and adulterates it by adding eighty-five gallons of pure spirits (refined whisky,) to fifteen gallons of brandy, to give it a flavor; then colors and “doctors” it, and it is ready for sale. Suppose an Albany wholesale-dealer purchases, for pure brandy, ten pipes of this adulterated brandy from a New York importer. The Albany man immediately doubles his stock by adding an equal quantity of pure spirits. There are then seven and a half gallons of brandy in a hundred. A Buffalo liquor-dealer buys from the Albany man, and he in turn adds one-half pure spirits. The Chicago dealer buys from the Buffalo dealer, and as nearly all spirit-dealers keep large quantities of pure spirits on hand, and know how to use it, he again doubles the quantity of his brandy by adding pure spirits; and the Milwaukee liquor-dealer does the same, after purchasing from the Chicago man. So, in the ordinary course of liquor transactions, by the time a hundred gallon pipe of pure brandy reaches Wisconsin, at a cost of five or perhaps ten dollars per gallon, ninety-nine gallons and one pint of it is the identical whisky that was shipped from Wisconsin the same year at fifty cents per gallon. Truly a homeoopathic dose of genuine brandy! And even that whisky when it left Wisconsin was only half whisky; for there are men in the whisky-making States who make it a business to take whisky direct from the distillery, add to it an equal quantity of water, and then bring it up to a bead and the power of intoxication, by mixing in a variety of the villainous drugs and deadly poisons enumerated in this chapter. The annual loss of strength, health, and life caused by the adulteration of liquor is truly appalling. Those who have not examined the subject can form no just estimate of the atrocious and extensive effects of this murderous humbug.

===Chinese===

我实在不知道有什么法子能确保买到任何种类的纯正酒水。有些人总喜欢趁葡萄酒和烈酒还锁在海关仓库里、尚未经进口商之手时就买下来。然而，纽约与费城却有好几十号人，靠着把美国威士忌运往法国，在当地精炼、上色、调味、做手脚，然后再当作法国白兰地运回纽约、缴清关税，赶在出海关之前脱手卖出，借此发了大财！法国某地出产一种特定牌子的葡萄酒，里头掺了红丹，每年当地都有或多或少的居民因直接从原产地源头喝了这种毒酒而患上“铅绞痛”。单是巴黎一城任何一年喝掉的假香槟，就比全世界一年酿出的真香槟还要多。美国通常每年消费的所谓香槟也多过全世界的产量，而实际上全世界几乎所有的真香槟都被欧洲各国宫廷包圆了。莱茵河畔约翰尼斯贝格（Johannisberg）出产的正宗霍克白葡萄酒（Hock），大宗批发价就值三美元一瓶，且几乎全数运往俄国；然而就在距离纯正压榨坊不到半英里的约翰尼斯贝格村任何一家旅馆里，只要花一美元一瓶，你就能买到号称正宗的霍克酒。自从化学技术让酒商得以用二十五美分到一美元一加仑的成本造出任何品类的葡萄酒或烈酒，每年制造并售出的成千上万加仑葡萄酒与白兰地，压根儿连葡萄味儿都没闻过。

试想某位批发酒商进口了一批纯正白兰地。他通常会进行“再蒸馏勾兑”和掺假：往十五加仑白兰地里兑入八十五加仑纯酒精（精制威士忌）来提味，再上色、做手脚，便可上市出售。再设想奥尔巴尼的一位批发商当成纯白兰地，从纽约进口商手里买下十大桶这种掺假白兰地。奥尔巴尼商人立刻兑入等量纯酒精，把存货翻了一倍。此时一百加仑里只剩七加仑半的白兰地。接着布法罗酒商从奥尔巴尼商人手里进货，照样再兑进一半纯酒精。芝加哥酒商从布法罗酒商手里买进，由于几乎所有烈酒商手头都囤着大批纯酒精且深谙其道，他又兑入纯酒精把白兰地总量翻了一倍；密尔沃基酒商从芝加哥人手里买来后如法炮制。于是，在寻常的酒业流转中，等一桶一百加仑的“纯正白兰地”以每加仑五美元甚至十美元的高价运抵威斯康星州时，其中九十九加仑零一品脱，恰恰正是同年以每加仑五十美分从威斯康星州运出去的那批威士忌原浆。这真可谓一剂货真价实的顺势疗法微剂量白兰地！而就连那批威士忌当年离开威斯康星时，也只有一半是真威士忌；因为产酒各州专有一批人以此为业：直接从蒸馏厂拉出威士忌，兑入等量清水，再掺进本章所列举的种种恶毒药剂与致命毒物，硬生生把酒花和醉人烈度勾兑出来。每年因酒类掺假而损耗的体力、健康与性命着实触目惊心。凡未曾深究此道之人，绝难估量这场谋财害命的欺罔所造成的滔天恶果与深广流毒。

### XX / 第二十章

===Original===

The Peter Funks and their functions﻿—​The rural divine and the watch﻿—​Rise and progress of mock auctions﻿—​Their decline and fall.
